\displaystyle\quad+\nabla W\ast(F^{1,N}-\tilde{\mu}^{N})\cdot\nabla_{v}(F^{1,N}-\mu)+\nabla W\ast(\tilde{\mu}^{N}-\mu)\cdot\nabla_{v}(F^{1,N}-\tilde{\mu}^{N}),

\displaystyle h^{2,N}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i\neq j}\nabla W(x_{i}-x_{j})\cdot\nabla_{v_{i}}(F^{2,N}-\mu^{\otimes 2})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{i}\Big(\nabla W\ast F^{1,N}(z_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v_{i}}(F^{1,N})^{\otimes 2}-\nabla W\ast\mu(x_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v_{i}}\mu^{\otimes 2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i}\nabla W\ast(\tfrac{N-2}{N}F^{1,N}-\mu)(x_{i})\cdot\nabla_{v_{i}}(NG^{2,N})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i\neq j}\nabla_{v_{i}}(\tfrac{N-2}{N}F^{1,N}-\mu)(z_{i})\cdot\int_{\mathbb{D}^{d}}\nabla W(x_{i}-x_{*})(NG^{2,N})(z_{j},z_{*})\,\mathrm{d}z_{*}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{i\neq j}F^{1,N}(z_{j})\int_{\mathbb{D}^{d}}\nabla W(x_{i}-x_{*})\cdot\nabla_{v_{i}}G^{2,N}(z_{i},z_{*})\,\mathrm{d}z_{*}

\displaystyle\quad+(N-2)\sum_{i}\int_{\mathbb{D}^{d}}\nabla W(x_{i}-x_{*})\cdot\nabla_{v_{i}}G^{3,N}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{*})\,\mathrm{d}z_{*}.

Note that
F^{1,N}|_{t=0}=\tilde{\mu}^{N}|_{t=0}=\mu_{\circ}
and
NG^{2,N}|_{t=0}=g^{(2)}|_{t=0}=0
.
Given
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq 1
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
, and
\kappa\ll_{W,\beta,a}1
, the ergodic estimates of Theorem
2.13
then yield for all
k\geq 2
and
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|F^{1,N}_{t}-\tilde{\mu}^{N}_{t}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a,\mu_{\circ}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-p\lambda_{0}(t-s)}\Big(N^{-1}\|NG^{2,N}_{s}-g^{(2)}_{s}\|_{W^{1-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\|\tilde{h}^{1,N}_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\|NG^{2,N}_{t}-g^{(2)}_{t}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a,\mu_{\circ}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-p\lambda_{0}(t-s)}\|h^{2,N}_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\mathrm{d}s.

By definition of
\tilde{h}^{1,N}
and
h^{2,N}
, appealing to the result (
4.3
) of Step 1, together with the correlation estimates of Theorem
1.1
, we find for all
k\gg 1
,
1<q\leq 2
, and
0<p\ll_{\theta}1
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
, provided
\kappa\ll_{W,\beta,p,a}1
,

\|\tilde{h}^{1,N}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-2},\qquad\|h^{2,N}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-1}.

Inserting this into the above, the conclusion follows.
∎

5.
Quantitative central limit theorem

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.4
. For
t\geq 0
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, consider the centered random variables

S^{N}_{t}(\phi)\,:=\,\sqrt{N}Y^{N}_{t}(\phi)\,:=\,\sqrt{N}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\mu^{N}_{t}-\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\mu^{N}_{t}\Big]\bigg).

We shall start by using a Lions expansion to split the contributions from initial data and from Brownian forces in the fluctuations.
From there, we separately analyze initial and Brownian fluctuations, using tools from Glauber and Lions calculus, respectively.

5.1.
Gaussian Dean–Kawasaki equation

We consider the Gaussian Dean–Kawasaki SPDE (
2
). With our general notation, covering the Langevin and Brownian settings at the same time, this reads as follows,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}\nu_{t}\,=\,L_{\mu_{t}}\nu_{t}+{\operatorname{div}}(\sqrt{\mu_{t}}\sigma_{0}\xi_{t}),&\text{for $t\geq 0$},\\
\nu_{t}|_{t=0}=\nu_{\circ},&\end{array}\right.

where:

—

L_{\mu}
is the linearized mean-field operator defined in (
2.18
);

—

\mu_{t}:=m(t,\mu_{\circ})
is the solution of the mean-field McKean–Vlasov equation (
1.28
);

—

\nu_{\circ}
is the Gaussian field describing the fluctuations of the initial empirical measure,
in the sense that
\sqrt{N}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi(\mu^{N}_{0}-\mu_{\circ})
converges in law to
\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\nu_{\circ}
for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
; in other words,
\nu_{\circ}
is the random tempered distribution on
\mathbb{X}
characterized by having Gaussian law with

(5.3)

\quad\operatorname{Var}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\nu_{\circ}\Big]\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(\phi-\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\mu_{\circ}\Big)^{2}\mu_{\circ},\qquad\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\nu_{\circ}\Big]\,=\,0,\qquad\text{for all $\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})$;}

—

\xi_{t}
is a Gaussian white noise on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{X}
and is taken independent of
\nu_{\circ}
.

As
\nu_{\circ}
and
\xi
are random tempered distributions on
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{X}
, respectively, equation (
5.1
) is naturally understood in the distributional sense almost surely, and its solution
\nu
must itself be sought as a random tempered distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{X}
.

In order to solve equation (
5.1
), we start by introducing some notation.
Given
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
,
k\geq 2
,
1<q\leq 2
, and
0<p\leq\frac{1}{4}\theta
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough, for all
h\in W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
, we can define
\{U_{t,s}[h]\}_{s\leq t}
as the unique weak solution in
C_{\operatorname{loc}}([s,\infty);W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p}))
of

(5.4)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}U_{t,s}[h]=L_{\mu_{t}}U_{t,s}[h],&\text{for $t\geq s$},\\
U_{t,s}[h]|_{t=s}=h,&\end{array}\right.

see Theorem
2.13
(ii).
We also consider the dual evolution
\{U_{t,s}^{*}\}_{t\geq s}
on
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})^{*}
, where for all
t\geq s
we define
U_{t,s}^{*}
as the adjoint of
U_{t,s}
,

\int_{\mathbb{X}}U_{t,s}^{*}[g]h\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}U_{t,s}[h]g.

Recall that the condition
pq^{\prime}>d
ensures that the dual space
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})^{*}
contains
W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{X})
.
For any
g\in W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})^{*}
and
t\geq 0
, the dual flow
s\mapsto U_{t,s}^{\ast}[g]
naturally belongs to
C([0,t];W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})^{*})
, where
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})^{*}
is endowed with the weak topology. Denoting by
L_{\mu}^{*}
the adjoint of the linearized mean-field operator
L_{\mu}
, we find that the dual flow satisfies the backward Cauchy problem

(5.5)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{s}U_{t,s}^{\ast}[g]=-L_{\mu_{s}}^{*}U_{t,s}^{*}[g],&\text{for $0\leq s\leq t$},\\
U_{t,s}^{\ast}[g]|_{s=t}=g.\end{array}\right.

In these terms, using the theory of Da Prato and Zabczyk
[
35
]
, and more specifically its non-autonomous extension by Seidler
[
88
]
, we can check that the Gaussian Dean–Kawasaki equation (
5.1
) admits a unique weak solution that is a random element in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X}))
, and it can be expressed by Duhamel’s principle

\nu_{t}\,:=\,U_{t,0}[\nu_{\circ}]+\int_{0}^{t}U_{t,s}\big[{\operatorname{div}}\big(\sqrt{\mu_{s}}\sigma_{0}\xi_{s}\big)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s.

In particular, the law of the solution is characterized by its covariance structure

(5.6)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\nu_{t}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{X}}U_{t,0}^{*}[\phi]\,\nu_{\circ}\bigg]+\operatorname{Var}\bigg[\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\sqrt{\mu_{s}}\sigma_{0}^{T}\nabla U_{t,s}^{*}[\phi]\cdot\xi_{s}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(U_{t,0}^{*}[\phi]-\int_{\mathbb{X}}U_{t,0}^{*}[\phi]\,\mu_{\circ}\Big)^{2}\mu_{\circ}\,+\,\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}\nabla U_{t,s}^{*}[\phi]\big|^{2}\mu_{s}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s.

5.2.
Splitting fluctuations

By means of a Lions expansion, we start by showing that fluctuations can be split neatly into contributions from initial data and from Brownian forces.

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists
\kappa_{1}\in(0,\kappa_{0}]
(only depending on
d,\beta,W,\theta,a
) such that given
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{1}]

we have for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
and
t\geq 0
,

\big\|S_{t}^{N}(\phi)-C_{t}^{N}(\phi)-D_{t}^{N}(\phi)\big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{X})},

in terms of

\displaystyle C_{t}^{N}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sqrt{N}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu^{N}_{0})-\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu^{N}_{0})\Big]\bigg),

\displaystyle D_{t}^{N}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{\mu}U(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})(Z^{i,N}_{s})\cdot\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B^{i}_{s},

where we have set for abbreviation
U(t,\mu):=\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu)
.

Proof.

Let
\kappa_{0},\lambda_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
and let
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq\frac{1}{4}\theta
be fixed with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough. For this value of
p
, using the notations of Theorem
2.13
(ii), we set
\kappa_{1}:=\min(\kappa_{2,p},\kappa_{3,p})
which belongs to
(0,\kappa_{0}]
and we let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{1}]
be fixed.
Starting point is the Lions expansion of Lemma
2.1
,

\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\mu^{N}_{t}\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{\mu}U(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})(Z^{i,N}_{s})\cdot\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B^{i}_{s}\\
+\frac{1}{2N}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\operatorname{tr}\Big[a_{0}\,\partial_{\mu}^{2}U(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})(z,z)\Big]\mu_{s}^{N}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Multiplying by
\sqrt{N}
, subtracting the expectation to both sides of the identity, taking the
\operatorname{L}^{2}(\Omega)
norm, and recognizing the definition of
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
and
D_{t}^{N}(\phi)
, we are led to

(5.7)

\big\|S_{t}^{N}(\phi)-C_{t}^{N}(\phi)-D_{t}^{N}(\phi)\big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\leq\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{N}}\bigg\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\operatorname{tr}\Big[a_{0}\,\partial_{\mu}^{2}U(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})(z,z)\Big]\mu_{s}^{N}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

By Lemma
3.6
, we get

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\operatorname{tr}\Big[a_{0}\,\partial_{\mu}^{2}U(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})(z,z)\Big]\mu_{s}^{N}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a}\,\|\phi\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-p\lambda_{0}(t-s)}Q(\mu^{N}_{s})^{2p}e^{4pQ(\mu^{N}_{s})^{\theta}}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Appealing to Lemma
2.12
together with the exponential moment assumption for
\mu_{\circ}
, we deduce

\bigg\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\operatorname{tr}\Big[a_{0}\,\partial_{\mu}^{2}U(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})(z,z)\Big]\mu_{s}^{N}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,\|\phi\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}.

Combined with (
5.7
), this yields the conclusion.
∎

5.3.
Initial fluctuations

We establish the following quantitative CLT for initial fluctuations
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
. The proof is split into two separate parts: the asymptotic normality of
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
and the convergence of its variance structure. For both parts, we exploit tools from Glauber calculus: more precisely, the first part follows from Stein’s method in form of the so-called second-order Poincaré inequality of Proposition
2.9
, while for the second part our starting point is the Helffer–Sjöstrand representation for the variance in Lemma
2.7
(iii).

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\lambda_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
,
\kappa_{1}
as in Lemma
5.1
, and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{1}]
be fixed.
The random variable
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
defined in Lemma
5.1
satisfies for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
and
t\geq 0
,

\mathrm{d}_{2}\Big({C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}\,,\,{\sigma^{C}_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}}\Big)\\[-5.69054pt]
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\theta\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}\bigg(1+\Big(\tfrac{\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})}{\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}}+(N^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\theta\lambda_{0}t})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)^{-1}\bigg),

where the limit variance is defined by

(5.8)

\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}\,:=\,\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}\!\big[(U_{t,0}^{*}[\phi])(Z_{\circ}^{1,N})\big]\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(U_{t,0}^{*}[\phi]-\int_{\mathbb{X}}U_{t,0}^{*}[\phi]\,\mu_{\circ}\Big)^{2}\mu_{\circ},

where we recall that
U_{t,0}^{*}
is defined in (
5.5
),
that
\mathrm{d}_{2}
is the second-order Zolotarev metric (
1.22
), and that
\mathcal{N}
stands for a standard normal random variable.

Proof.

Let
1<q\leq 2
,
0<p\leq\frac{1}{4}\theta
, and
\kappa_{1}
be fixed as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
, and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{1}]
.
We split the proof into three steps.

Step
1. Asymptotic normality: proof that for all
t\geq 0
,

(5.9)

\mathrm{d}_{2}\bigg(\frac{C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}{\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,,\,\mathcal{N}\bigg)\,+\,\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{W}}\bigg(\frac{C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}{\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,,\,\mathcal{N}\bigg)\,+\,\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{K}}\bigg(\frac{C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}{\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,,\,\mathcal{N}\bigg)\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{-1}\Big(1+\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

Set for abbreviation

\hat{C}_{t}^{N}(\phi)\,:=\,\frac{C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}{\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

By Proposition
2.9
, we can estimate

\mathrm{d}_{2}\big(\hat{C}_{t}^{N}(\phi),\mathcal{N}\big)\,+\,\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{W}}\big(\hat{C}_{t}^{N}(\phi),\mathcal{N}\big)\,+\,\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{K}}\big(\hat{C}_{t}^{N}(\phi),\mathcal{N}\big)\,\lesssim\,\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{-\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[{|D^{\circ}_{j}C_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{6}}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
+\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[{|D^{\circ}_{l}C_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{4}}\big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[{|D^{\circ}_{j}D^{\circ}_{l}C_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{4}}\big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big)^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Recalling the definition of
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
in Lemma
5.1
, using (
2.13
) and (
2.14
) to bound Glauber derivatives by means of linear derivatives, appealing to Lemma
3.6
to estimate the latter, and distinguishing between the cases
l=j
and
l\neq j
in the second right-hand side term, we deduce

\mathrm{d}_{2}\big(\hat{C}_{t}^{N}(\phi),\mathcal{N}\big)\,+\,\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{W}}\big(\hat{C}_{t}^{N}(\phi),\mathcal{N}\big)\,+\,\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{K}}\big(\hat{C}_{t}^{N}(\phi),\mathcal{N}\big)\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a}\,\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{-\frac{3}{2}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-3p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{3}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[e^{CpQ(\mu^{N}_{0})^{\theta}}\big]\\
+\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{-1}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[e^{CpQ(\mu^{N}_{0})^{\theta}}\big].

and the claim follows from the exponential moment assumption on
\mu_{\circ}
.

Step
2. Convergence of the variance: proof that for all
t\geq 0
,

(5.10)

\Big|\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]-\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}\Big|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-1}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2},

where the limit variance is defined in (
5.8
).

By the definition of
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
in Lemma
5.1
, we have

\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]\,=\,N\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big].

Appealing to the Helffer–Sjöstrand representation for the variance in terms of Glauber calculus, cf. Lemma
2.7
(iii), we get

\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]\,=\,N\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\Big(D^{\circ}_{j}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big)\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}\Big(D^{\circ}_{j}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big)\bigg].

By exchangeability, this is equivalently written as

\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]\,=\,N^{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\Big(D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big)\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}\Big(D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big)\bigg].

Using the short-hand notation
\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{1}:=\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[\,\cdot\,|\{Z_{\circ}^{i,N}\}_{2\leq i\leq N}]
,
\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}:=\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[\,\cdot\,|Z_{\circ}^{1,N}]
, and
\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}:=\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[\,\cdot\,|Z_{\circ}^{1,N}]
, and noting that
\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}D_{1}^{\circ}=\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}D_{1}^{\circ}
, we deduce from the triangle inequality

\bigg|\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]-N^{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}\Big[D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big]\bigg)\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}\Big[D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big]\bigg)\bigg]\bigg|\\
\,\leq\,N^{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{1}\bigg[\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}\Big[D_{1}^{\circ}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big]\bigg].

Since we have
\mathcal{L}_{\circ}X=X
for any
\sigma(Z_{\circ}^{1,N})
-measurable random variable
X
with
\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[X]=0
, the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}
can be replaced by
\operatorname{Id}
in the left-hand side.
Further appealing to the variance inequality (
2.12
) for Glauber calculus, we are led to

\bigg|\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]-N^{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}\Big[D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big]^{2}\bigg]\bigg|\,\leq\,N^{3}\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\Big|D^{\circ}_{2}D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big|^{2}\bigg].

Using (
2.14
) to bound Glauber derivatives by means of linear derivatives, appealing to Lemma
3.6
to estimate the latter, and recalling the exponential moment assumption for
\mu_{\circ}
, we obtain

(5.11)

\bigg|\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]-N^{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{\neq 1}\Big[D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big]^{2}\bigg]\bigg|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-1}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}.

It remains to evaluate the Glauber derivative in the left-hand side.
Recalling again the link between Glauber and linear derivatives, cf. (
2.13
), and appealing to Lemma
3.6
for the computation of the linear derivative, we get

(5.12)

{D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu^{N}_{0})}\\
\,=\,N^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m^{(1)}\big(t,\mu_{0}^{N}+\tfrac{1-s}{N}(\delta_{z}-\delta_{Z_{\circ}^{1,N}}),y\big)\bigg)\,(\delta_{Z_{\circ}^{1,N}}-\delta_{z})(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mu_{\circ}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Let us further appeal to the definition of linear derivative to replace the measure
\mu_{0}^{N}+\frac{1-s}{N}(\delta_{z}-\delta_{Z_{\circ}^{1,N}})
in the argument of
m^{(1)}
by

\mu_{0,z^{\prime}}^{N}\,:=\,\mu_{0}^{N}+\tfrac{1}{N}(\delta_{z^{\prime}}-\delta_{Z_{\circ}^{1,N}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{N}\delta_{z^{\prime}}+\tfrac{1}{N}\textstyle\sum_{j=2}^{N}\delta_{Z_{\circ}^{j,N}},

where
z^{\prime}
is a new variable integrated over with respect to
\mu_{\circ}
.
Using Lemma
3.6
to estimate the additional linear derivative that constitutes the resulting error, we get

\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\Big|{D^{\circ}_{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu^{N}_{0})}-N^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m^{(1)}(t,\mu_{0,z^{\prime}}^{N},y)\Big)\,(\delta_{Z_{\circ}^{1,N}}-\mu_{\circ})(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mu_{\circ}(\mathrm{d}z^{\prime})\Big|^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-2}e^{-p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}.

Inserting this into (
5.11
) and reorganizing expectations and integrals, we obtain

\bigg|\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]-\int_{\mathbb{X}}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m^{(1)}(t,\mu_{0}^{N},y)\Big)\,(\delta_{z}-\mu_{\circ})(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg]^{2}\,\mu_{\circ}(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg|\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-1}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}.

We are now in position to appeal to Lemma
2.3
to replace
\mu_{0}^{N}
by
\mu_{\circ}
in the expectation. Using as before Lemma
3.6
and the exponential moment assumption for
\mu_{\circ}
to estimate linear derivatives, this leads us to

(5.13)

\bigg|\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]-\int_{\mathbb{X}}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m^{(1)}(t,\mu_{\circ},y)\Big)\,(\delta_{z}-\mu_{\circ})(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg)^{2}\,\mu_{\circ}(\mathrm{d}z)\bigg|\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-1}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}.

Using the notation (
5.4
), the definition of
m^{(1)}
in Lemma
3.6
amounts to

m^{(1)}(t,\mu_{\circ},y)=U_{t,0}[\delta_{y}-\mu_{\circ}],

and the claim (
5.10
) follows with the limit variance
\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})
defined in (
5.8
).

Step
3. Conclusion.

By homogeneity of
\mathrm{d}_{2}
and by the triangle inequality, we can estimate

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{2}\Big({C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}\,,\,{\sigma^{C}_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]\,\mathrm{d}_{2}\bigg(\frac{C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}{\operatorname{Var}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{1/2}}\,,\,\frac{\sigma^{C}_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})}{\operatorname{Var}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{1/2}}\mathcal{N}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]\,\mathrm{d}_{2}\bigg(\frac{C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}{\operatorname{Var}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{1/2}}\,,\,\mathcal{N}\bigg)+\tfrac{1}{2}\Big|\operatorname{Var}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]-\sigma^{C}_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}\Big|.

By the asymptotic normality (
5.9
) and by the convergence result (
5.10
) for the variance, we then get

(5.14)

\mathrm{d}_{2}\Big({C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}\,,\,{\sigma^{C}_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}}\Big)\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\Big(1+\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}\operatorname{Var}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

It remains to deal with the last factor involving the inverse of the variance.
For that purpose, we distinguish between two cases:

—

Case 1:
\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}\geq L\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}
.

In this case, the convergence result (
5.10
) for the variance yields

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}\operatorname{Var}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}\Big(\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}-CN^{-1}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\Big)_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(L-CN^{-1}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t})_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}},

so that (
5.14
) becomes

\mathrm{d}_{2}\Big({C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}\,,\,{\sigma^{C}_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}}\Big)\\
\,\lesssim\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t}\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\Big(1+(L-CN^{-1}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t})_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

—

Case 2:
\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}\leq L\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}
.

In this case, the convergence result (
5.10
) for the variance yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{2}\Big({C_{t}^{N}(\phi)}\,,\,{\sigma^{C}_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sigma^{C}_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}+\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[C_{t}^{N}(\phi)]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2L+CN^{-1}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}t})\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}.

Optimizing between those two cases and choosing
p=\frac{1}{4}\theta
, the conclusion follows.
∎

5.4.
Brownian fluctuations

We establish the following quantitative CLT for Brownian fluctuations
D_{t}^{N}(\phi)
.
The proof is based on the Lions expansion combined with a simple heat-kernel PDE argument.
The additional estimate (
5.17
) in the statement below ensures that the CLT for
D_{t}^{N}(\phi)
also holds given
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
, which is key to deduce a joint result.

Lemma 5.3

(Brownian fluctuations)
.

Let
\kappa_{1}
be as in Lemma
5.1

and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{1}]
be fixed. The random variable
D_{t}^{N}(\phi)
defined in Lemma
5.1
satisfies for all
t\geq 0
,

(5.15)

\mathrm{d}_{2}\big(\,D_{t}^{N}(\phi)\,,\,\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\,\mathcal{N}\,\big)\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2},

where the limit variance is given by

(5.16)

\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}\,:=\,\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}\nabla U_{t,s}^{*}[\phi])\big|^{2}\,m(s,\mu_{\circ})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

where we recall that
U_{t,s}^{*}
is defined in (
5.5
),
that
\mathrm{d}_{2}
is the second-order Zolotarev metric (
1.22
), and that
\mathcal{N}
stands for a standard normal random variable.
In addition, for all
h\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
t\geq 0
,

(5.17)

\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[h\big(C_{t}^{N}(\phi),D_{t}^{N}(\phi)\big)\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\big[h\big(C_{t}^{N}(\phi),\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}\big)\big]\Big|\\[-2.84526pt]
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\|\partial_{2}^{2}h\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where the standard normal variable
\mathcal{N}
is taken independent both of initial data and of Brownian forces, and where we denote by
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}
the expectation with respect to
\mathcal{N}
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
,
let
1<q\leq 2
,
0<p\leq\frac{1}{4}\theta
, and
\kappa_{1}
be fixed as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
, and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{1}]
.
We focus on the proof of (
5.15
), while the additional statement (
5.17
) can be obtained along the exact same lines — simply replacing the test function
g
below by
h(C_{t}^{N}(\phi),\cdot)
and recalling that
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
is independent of Brownian forces.
We split the proof into two steps.

Step
1. Proof that for all
g\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
and
t\geq 0
,

(5.18)

\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[g(D_{t}^{N}(\phi))\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\Big[g\big(\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})\,\mathcal{N}\big)\Big]\Big|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})},

where the limit variance is defined in (
5.16
),
and where as in the statement
\mathcal{N}
stands for a standard normal random variable taken independent both of initial data and of Brownian forces.

To prove this result, let us consider the
(\mathcal{F}^{B}_{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
-martingale
(D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi))_{0\leq s\leq t}
given by

D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi)\,:=\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{s}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-u,\mu_{u}^{N})(Z^{i,N}_{u})\cdot\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{u}^{i},

which satisfies

D_{0,t}^{N}(\phi)=0,\qquad D_{t,t}^{N}(\phi)=D_{t}^{N}(\phi).

Let
g\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
be fixed. By definition of
D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi)
, Itô’s lemma yields for all
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
and
0\leq s\leq t
,

(5.19)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[g(\rho+D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi))\big]\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{B}\bigg[g^{\prime\prime}(\rho+D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi))\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})\big|^{2}\,\mu_{s}^{N}\bigg].

Appealing to the Lions expansion in form of Corollary
2.2
(ii), we find for all
0\leq s\leq t
,

\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})\big|^{2}\,\mu_{s}^{N}-\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-s,m(s,\mu_{0}^{N}))\big|^{2}\,m(s,\mu_{0}^{N})\bigg\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\Omega_{B})}\\
\,\lesssim\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{B}\bigg[\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\partial_{\mu}H_{t-s}(s-u,\mu_{u}^{N})(z)\big|^{2}\mu_{u}^{N}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}u\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
+N^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{B}\bigg[\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\partial_{\mu}^{2}H_{t-s}(s-u,\mu_{u}^{N})(z,z)\big|\,\mu_{u}^{N}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{2}\bigg],

in terms of

H_{t-s}(u,\mu)\,:=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big|\sigma_{0}^{T}\partial_{\mu}U(t-s,m(u,\mu))\Big|^{2}m(u,\mu).

Appealing to Lemma
3.6
to estimate the multiple linear derivatives,
together with the moment bounds of Lemma
2.12
, we get after straightforward computations for all
0\leq s\leq t
,

\bigg\|\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})\big|^{2}\,\mu_{s}^{N}-\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-s,m(s,\mu_{0}^{N}))\big|^{2}\,m(s,\mu_{0}^{N})\bigg\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\Omega_{B})}\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-p\lambda_{0}(t-s)}e^{CpQ(\mu^{N}_{0})^{\theta}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}.

Inserting this into (
5.19
), and using the short-hand notation

\kappa_{s,t}(\phi,\mu)\,:=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-s,m(s,\mu))\big|^{2}\,m(s,\mu),

we deduce for all
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq s\leq t
, and
\lambda\in[0,\frac{1}{8}\lambda_{0})
,

\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[g(\rho+D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi))\big]-\tfrac{1}{2}\kappa_{s,t}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})\,\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}\rho^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[g(\rho+D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi))\big]\bigg|\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-p\lambda_{0}(t-s)}e^{CpQ(\mu^{N}_{0})^{\theta}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

In order to solve this approximate heat equation for the map
(s,\rho)\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{B}[g(\rho+D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi))]
on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}
,
we can appeal for instance to the Feynman–Kac formula with
D_{0,t}^{N}(\phi)=0
. Equivalently, this amounts to integrating the above estimate with the associated heat kernel. It leads us to deduce for all
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
and
0\leq s\leq t
,

\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[g(\rho+D_{s,t}^{N}(\phi))\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\Big[g\Big(\rho+\mathcal{N}\sqrt{\textstyle\int_{0}^{s}\kappa_{u,t}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})\,\mathrm{d}u}\Big)\Big]\bigg|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{CpQ(\mu_{0}^{N})}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

In particular, setting
\rho=0
and
s=t
, recalling
D_{t,t}^{N}(\phi)=D_{t}^{N}(\phi)
, taking the expectation with respect to initial data, and recalling the exponential moment assumption for
\mu_{\circ}
, we get

\bigg|\mathbb{E}\big[g(D_{t}^{N}(\phi))\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\Big[g\Big(\mathcal{N}\sqrt{\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\kappa_{u,t}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})\,\mathrm{d}u}\Big)\Big]\bigg|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,p,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\,\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Using the notation (
5.4
) and recalling that the definition of
m^{(1)}
in Lemma
3.6
amounts to

m^{(1)}(t,\mu_{\circ},y)=U_{t,0}[\delta_{y}-\mu_{\circ}],

we recognize the definition (
5.16
) of
\sigma_{t}^{D}
,

(5.20)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\kappa_{u,t}(\phi,\mu)\,\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-s,m(s,\mu))\big|^{2}\,m(s,\mu)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}\nabla U_{t,s}^{*}[\phi])\big|^{2}\,m(s,\mu)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu)^{2},

and the claim (
5.18
) follows.

Step
2. Proof that for all
g\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
and
t\geq 0
,

(5.21)

\Big|\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\Big[g\big(\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})\,\mathcal{N}\big)\Big]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\Big[g\big(\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\,\mathcal{N}\big)\Big]\Big|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Combining this with the result (
5.18
) of Step 1, and recalling the definition (
1.22
) of the second-order Zolotarev metric, this will conclude the proof of (
5.15
).
Set for shortness
\sigma_{t}^{N}:=\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})
and
\sigma_{t}:=\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})
.
We can decompose

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\big[g(\sigma_{t}^{N}\mathcal{N})\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\big[g(\sigma_{t}\,\mathcal{N})\big]\,=\,(\sigma_{t}^{N}-\sigma_{t})\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\Big[\mathcal{N}g^{\prime}\Big(\big(\sigma_{t}+\rho(\sigma_{t}^{N}-\sigma_{t})\big)\mathcal{N}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\rho,

and a Gaussian integration by parts then yields

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\big[g(\sigma_{t}^{N}\mathcal{N})\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\big[g(\sigma_{t}\,\mathcal{N})\big]\,=\,(\sigma_{t}^{N}-\sigma_{t})\int_{0}^{1}\big(\sigma_{t}+\rho(\sigma_{t}^{N}-\sigma_{t})\big)\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\Big[g^{\prime\prime}\Big(\big(\sigma_{t}+\rho(\sigma_{t}^{N}-\sigma_{t})\big)\mathcal{N}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\rho.

Hence,

\Big|\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\big[g(\sigma_{t}^{N}\mathcal{N})\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\big[g(\sigma_{t}\,\mathcal{N})\big]\Big|\,\leq\,|\sigma_{t}^{N}-\sigma_{t}|\big(|\sigma_{t}|+|\sigma_{t}^{N}-\sigma_{t}|\big)\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Taking the expectation with respect to initial data, the claim (
5.21
) would follow provided that we could show for all
t\geq 0
,

(5.22)

\displaystyle|\sigma_{t}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})},

(5.23)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[|\sigma_{t}^{N}-\sigma_{t}|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}

\displaystyle N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}.

For that purpose, we first recall that by (
5.20
) we can write

(5.24)

\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu)^{2}\,=\,\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big|\sigma_{0}^{T}(\partial_{\mu}U)(t-s,m(s,\mu))\big|^{2}\,m(s,\mu)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

with the short-hand notation
U(t,\mu):=\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu)
.
Applying Lemma
3.6
to estimate the linear derivative, and combining it with the moment bounds of Lemma
2.12
, the claim (
5.22
) follows after straightforward computations.
We turn to the proof of (
5.23
).
By the triangle inequality, we can decompose

\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[|\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})-\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})|^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\leq\,\big|\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})]-\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\big|+\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})]^{\frac{1}{2}},

and we estimate both terms separately.
On the one hand, starting again from (
5.24
), appealing to Lemma
2.3
, using Lemma
3.6
to estimate the multiple linear derivatives, and combining it with the bounds of Lemma
2.12
and with the exponential moment assumption for
\mu_{\circ}
, we easily get

\big|\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})]-\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\big|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-1}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}.

On the other hand, using the variance inequality (
2.12
) for Glauber calculus, and appealing to (
2.13
) to bound Glauber derivatives in terms of linear derivatives, we find

\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}\big[\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})\big]\,\leq\,\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[|D^{\circ}_{j}\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})|^{2}\big]\\
\,\lesssim\,N^{-1}\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\frac{\delta{\sigma_{t}^{D}}}{\delta\mu}\Big(\phi,\mu^{N}_{0}+\tfrac{1-s}{N}(\delta_{z}-\delta_{Z_{0}^{1,N}}),y\Big)\,(\delta_{Z^{1,N}_{0}}-\delta_{z})(\mathrm{d}y)\,\mu_{\circ}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|^{2}\bigg].

Further using Lemma
3.6
to estimate the multiple linear derivatives, and combining it with the subgaussian bounds of Lemma
2.12
and with the exponential moment assumption for
\mu_{\circ}
, we deduce

\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{0}^{N})]\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-1}\|\phi\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2},

and the claim (
5.23
) follows.
∎

5.5.
Proof of Theorem
1.4

Let
\lambda_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
,
\kappa_{1}
as in Lemma
5.1
, and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{1}]
be fixed.
Let also
g\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
be momentarily fixed with
g^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}=1
.
By Lemma
5.1
, we find

\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[g(S_{t}^{N}(\phi))\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[g\big(C_{t}^{N}(\phi)+D_{t}^{N}(\phi)\big)\big]\Big|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}.

Next, appealing to (
5.17
) in Lemma
5.3
for the asymptotic normality of
D_{t}^{N}(\phi)
given
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
, we deduce

(5.25)

\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[g(S_{t}^{N}(\phi))\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\big[g\big(C_{t}^{N}(\phi)+\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}\big)\big]\Big|\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2},

where
\mathcal{N}
stands for a standard normal random variable taken independent both of initial data and of Brownian forces.
It remains to combine this with our analysis of fluctuations of
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
.
Appealing to the asymptotic normality of
C_{t}^{N}(\phi)
as stated in Lemma
5.2
, and testing that result in Zolotarev metric with the function
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}[g(\cdot+\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N})]\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
, we deduce

\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[g(S_{t}^{N}(\phi))\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}\big[g\big(\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}^{\prime}+\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}\big)\big]\Big|\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}\,N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\bigg(1+e^{-\frac{1}{2}\theta\lambda_{0}t}\Big(\tfrac{\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})}{\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}}+(N^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\theta\lambda_{0}t})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)^{-1}\bigg),

where
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
stands for another standard normal random variable taken independent of initial data, of Brownian forces, and of
\mathcal{N}
.
Taking the supremum over
g
, and noting that
\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}^{\prime}+\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}
has the same distribution as
\sigma_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}
with total variance

\sigma_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}\,:=\,\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{D}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})^{2},

we conclude

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{2}\Big(S_{t}^{N}(\phi)\,,\,\sigma_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})\mathcal{N}\Big)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \lesssim_{W,\beta,a,\mu_{\circ}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\bigg(1+e^{-\frac{1}{2}\theta\lambda_{0}t}\Big(\tfrac{\sigma_{t}^{C}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})}{\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}}+(N^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\theta\lambda_{0}t})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)^{-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}+N^{-\frac{1}{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\theta\lambda_{0}t}\Big)\|\phi\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}.

Noting that the total variance
\sigma_{t}(\phi,\mu_{\circ})
coincides with the variance predicted by the Gaussian Dean–Kawasaki equation, cf. (
5.6
), the conclusion follows.∎

6.
Concentration estimates

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.7
. As the initial law
\mu_{\circ}
is assumed here to have compact support, we can choose e.g.
\theta=1
. Let
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq\frac{1}{4}
be fixed with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
. Using the notation
\kappa_{\ell,p}
from Theorem
2.13
(ii), we set
\kappa_{1}:=\min(\kappa_{2,p},\kappa_{3,p})\in(0,\kappa_{0}]
, and we let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{1}]
be fixed.
Let also
\lambda_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
.
For
t\geq 0
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, consider the centered random variables

Y_{t}^{N}(\phi)\,:=\,X_{t}^{N}(\phi)-\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{N}(\phi)]\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\mu_{t}^{N}-\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\mu_{t}^{N}\Big].

We establish concentration by means of moment estimates.
By the Lions expansion of Lemma
2.1
, we can decompose

Y_{t}^{N}(\phi)\,=\,\tilde{Y}_{t}^{N}(\phi)+M_{t}^{N}(\phi)+\frac{1}{2N}\big(E_{t}^{N}(\phi)-\mathbb{E}[E_{t}^{N}(\phi)]\big),

in terms of

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}_{t}^{N}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})-\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t,\mu_{0}^{N})\Big],

\displaystyle M_{t}^{N}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{\mu}U(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})(Z_{s}^{i,N})\cdot\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{i},

\displaystyle E_{t}^{N}(\phi)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\operatorname{tr}\Big[a_{0}\partial_{\mu}^{2}U(t-s,\mu_{s}^{N})(z,z)\Big]\mu_{s}^{N}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\mathrm{d}s,

where we use the short-hand notation

U(t-s,\mu)\,:=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,m(t-s,\mu).

For all
k\geq 1
, we may then decompose the
k
th moment of
Y_{t}^{N}(\phi)
as

(6.1)

\mathbb{E}\big[|Y_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}\big]\,\leq\,3^{k}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[|\tilde{Y}_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}\big]+3^{k}\mathbb{E}\big[|M_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}\big]+3^{k}N^{-k}\mathbb{E}\big[|E_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}\big].

We separately analyze the three right-hand side terms and split the proof into four steps.
In the sequel, all constants
C
are implicitly allowed to depend on
W,\beta,p,q,a
, as well as on the support of
\mu_{\circ}
, which is assumed here to be compact.

Step
1. Proof that for all
1\leq k\leq N
,

(6.2)

\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[|\tilde{Y}_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}\big]\,\leq\,\Big(\frac{Ck}{N}\Big)^{\frac{k}{2}}e^{-kp\lambda_{0}t}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{2,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{k}.

Using (
2.14
) to estimate the Glauber derivative by means of a linear derivative, using Lemma
3.6
to control the latter, and noting that the support assumption for
\mu_{\circ}
ensures that
Q(\mu_{0}^{N})
and
|Z_{0}^{j,N}|
are uniformly bounded almost surely, we get

|D^{\circ}_{j}\tilde{Y}_{t}^{N}(\phi)|\,\leq\,CN^{-1}e^{-p\lambda_{0}t}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{2,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}.

We may then appeal to Proposition
2.10
, to the effect of

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[|\tilde{Y}_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda>0}\Big\{k!\lambda^{-k}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[e^{\lambda\tilde{Y}_{t}^{N}(\phi)}\big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda>0}\Big\{k!\lambda^{-k}\exp\Big(C\lambda e^{-p\lambda_{0}t}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{2,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}\big(e^{2\lambda CN^{-1}e^{-p\lambda_{0}t}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{2,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}}-1\big)\Big)\Big\}.

Choosing
\lambda=(kN)^{1/2}(2Ce^{-p\lambda_{0}t}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{2,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})})^{-1}
, the claim (
6.2
) follows.

Step
2. Proof that for all
1\leq k\leq N
,

(6.3)

\mathbb{E}[|M^{N}_{t}(\phi)|^{k}]\,\leq\,\Big(\frac{Ck}{N}\Big)^{\frac{k}{2}}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{2,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{k}.

We recall that
(M^{N}_{t}(\phi))_{t}
is a martingale with quadratic variation given by

\langle M^{N}_{t}(\phi)\rangle\,=\,\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\big|\partial_{\mu}U(t-s,\mu^{N}_{s})(Z^{i,N}_{s})\big|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Appealing to Lemma
3.6
to estimate the L-derivative, we get with our choice of
p,q,\kappa
,

\langle M^{N}_{t}(\phi)\rangle\,\leq\,CN^{-1}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{2,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2p\lambda_{0}(t-s)}Q(\mu_{s}^{N})^{2p}e^{4pQ(\mu^{N}_{s})}\mathrm{d}s.

By the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality, see e.g.
[
96
, Theorem 1]
, we have for all
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}[|M^{N}_{t}(\phi)|^{k}]\,\leq\,(Ck)^{\frac{k}{2}}\mathbb{E}[\langle M^{N}_{t}(\phi)\rangle^{\frac{k}{2}}],

and thus

\mathbb{E}[|M^{N}_{t}(\phi)|^{k}]\,\leq\,\Big(\frac{Ck}{N}\Big)^{\frac{k}{2}}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{2,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{k}\max_{0\leq s\leq t}\mathbb{E}\big[Q(\mu^{N}_{s})^{kp}e^{2kpQ(\mu^{N}_{s})}\big].

Appealing to Lemma
2.12
together with the compact support assumption on
\mu_{\circ}
, we find for
s\geq 0
,

\mathbb{E}\big[Q(\mu^{N}_{s})^{kp}e^{2kpQ(\mu^{N}_{s})}\big]\,\leq\,\mathbb{E}\big[e^{3kpQ(\mu^{N}_{s})}\big]\,\leq\,e^{Ck^{2}/N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[e^{CkQ(\mu^{N}_{0})}\big]\,\lesssim\,e^{Ck+Ck^{2}/N},

and the claim (
6.3
) follows.

Step
3. Proof that for all
1\leq k\leq N
,

(6.4)

\mathbb{E}[|E_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}]\,\leq\,C^{k}\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{3,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{k}.

Recalling the definition of
E_{t}^{N}(\phi)
, and appealing to Lemma
3.6
to estimate multiple L-derivatives, we get

|E_{t}^{N}(\phi)|\,\lesssim\,\|\langle z\rangle^{-p}\phi\|_{W^{3,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-p\lambda_{0}(t-s)}Q(\mu^{N}_{s})^{2p}e^{4pQ(\mu^{N}_{s})}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Now appealing to the moment bounds of Lemma
2.12
, together with Jensen’s inequality and with the compact support assumption for
\mu_{\circ}
, the claim (
6.4
) follows.

Step
4. Conclusion.

Inserting the results of the first three steps into (
6.1
), we obtain for all
1\leq k\leq N
,

\mathbb{E}[|Y_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}]\,\leq\,\Big(\frac{Ck}{N}\Big)^{\frac{k}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{k}.

Combining with the trivial bound
|Y_{t}^{N}(\phi)|\leq 2\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})}
, we find that the above estimate actually holds for all
k\geq 1
(without the restriction to
k\leq N
).
For
r\geq 0
, we may then deduce by Markov’s inequality, for all
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\left[Y_{t}^{N}(\phi)\geq r\right]\,\leq\,r^{-k}\,\mathbb{E}[|Y_{t}^{N}(\phi)|^{k}]\,\leq\,\bigg(\frac{Ck}{Nr^{2}}\bigg)^{\frac{k}{2}}\|\phi\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{k}.

Choosing

k=Nr^{2}\big(eC\|\phi\|_{W^{3,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\big)^{-1}
,
the conclusion follows.
∎

7.
Ergodic Sobolev estimates for mean field

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
2.13
.
As recalled in Section
2.5
, the convergence to equilibrium in item (i) of the statement is well known.
We focus here on the proof of the ergodic estimates in item (ii) and we restrict to the kinetic Langevin setting (
1.30
), while we emphasize that the same arguments can be repeated and substantially simplified in the Brownian setting (in which case the estimates further hold on the unweighted spaces
W^{-k,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
).

As stated in item (i), recall that the mean-field evolution (
1.11
) has a unique steady state
M
for
\kappa
in
[0,\kappa_{0}]
, which can be characterized in the Langevin setting as the unique solution of the fixed-point Gibbs equation

M(x,v)\,=\,c_{M}\exp\Big[-\beta\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}|v|^{2}+A(x)+\kappa W\ast M(x)\Big)\Big],\qquad z=(x,v)\in\mathbb{X},

where
c_{M}
is the normalizing constant such that
\int_{\mathbb{X}}M=1
. Note that this fixed-point equation has indeed a unique solution provided that
\kappa\beta\|W\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<1
.

7.1.
Exponential relaxation for modified linearized operators

We start by considering the following modified version of the linearized operator
L_{\mu}
defined in (
2.19
), where we remove the (compact) convolution term:
given a measure
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{X})
, we define for all
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,

(7.1)

R_{\mu}h\,:=\,\tfrac{1}{2}{\operatorname{div}}_{v}((\nabla_{v}+\beta v)h)-v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+(\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu)\cdot\nabla_{v}h.

Given
\mu_{\circ}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{X})
,
s\geq 0
, and
h_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, recalling that
\mu_{t}:=m(t,\mu_{\circ})
stands for the solution of the mean-field equation (
1.11
),
we consider the following (non-autonomous) equation,

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}h_{t}=R_{\mu_{t}}h_{t},\quad\text{for $t\geq s$},\\
h_{t}|_{t=s}=h_{s}.\end{array}\right.

It is easily checked that this linear parabolic equation is well-posed with
h\in C_{\operatorname{loc}}([s,\infty);\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2}))
whenever the initial condition
h_{s}
belongs to
\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})
.
We then consider the associated fundamental solution operators
\{V_{t,s}\}_{t\geq s\geq 0}
on
\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})
defined by

(7.2)

h_{t}\,=\,V_{t,s}h_{s}.

Note that
\int_{\mathbb{X}}V_{t,s}h_{s}=\int_{\mathbb{X}}h_{s}
for all
t\geq s
.
We establish the following exponential convergence result to the steady state.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\kappa_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
(i) and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
.
There exists
\lambda_{1}>0
(only depending on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
) such that the following holds: given
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq 1
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,a
), we have for all
k\geq 0
,
h_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,
t\geq s\geq 0
, and
\delta\in[0,1]
,

\Big\|V_{t,s}h_{s}-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}h_{s}\Big\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a}\,e^{-(\delta\wedge p)\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\Big(1+e^{-\delta\lambda_{1}s}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{k}}\Big)\|h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})},

for some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
,
and where the multiplicative constant further depends on
p,q
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+d+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

To prove this result, we shall first establish the exponential decay on negative Sobolev spaces with the Gibbs weight
M
, that is, the exponential decay on the smaller spaces
H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})
, and next we shall appeal to the enlargement theory of Gualdani, Mischler and Mouhot
[
57
,
80
]
to conclude with the desired result on
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
. Here, for all
k\geq 0
, the space
H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})
is defined as the weighted negative Sobolev space associated with the dual norm

(7.3)

\|h\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,:=\,\sup\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{X}}hh^{\prime}M^{-1}\,:\,\|h^{\prime}\|_{H^{k}(M^{-1/2})}=1\bigg\},

where
H^{k}(M^{-1/2})
is the standard weighted Sobolev space with norm

\|h\|_{H^{k}(M^{-1/2})}\,:=\,\sup_{i,j\geq 0,\,i+j\leq k}\|\nabla_{x}^{i}\nabla_{v}^{j}h\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})},\qquad\|h\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}\,:=\,\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}|h|^{2}M^{-1}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Note that the treatment of the weight in the definition of those weighted spaces differs slightly from the one in the definition of
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
, cf. (
2.20
), but for convenience we stick to this slight inconsistency in the choice of definitions.

In order to appeal to enlargement theory, we start by introducing a suitable decomposition of the operator
R_{\mu}
.
Let a cut-off function
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
be fixed with
\chi(z)=1
for
|z|\leq 1
, and set

(7.4)

\chi_{R}(z):=\chi(\tfrac{1}{R}z).

In those terms, we split the operator
R_{\mu}
as follows,

(7.5)

\displaystyle R_{\mu}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}+B_{\mu},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}h

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Lambda\chi_{R}h,

\displaystyle B_{\mu}h

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}{\operatorname{div}}_{v}((\nabla_{v}+\beta v)h)-v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+(\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu)\cdot\nabla_{v}h-\Lambda\chi_{R}h,

for some constants
\Lambda,R>0
to be properly chosen later on (see Lemmas
7.3
and
7.4
below).
Let us denote by
\{W_{t,s}\}_{t\geq s\geq 0}
the fundamental solution operators for the (non-autonomous) evolution equation associated with
B_{\mu}
: for all
s\geq 0
and
h_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, we define
h_{t}=W_{t,s}h_{s}
as the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}h_{t}=B_{\mu_{t}}h_{t},\quad\text{for $t\geq s$},\\
h_{t}|_{t=s}=h_{s}.\end{array}\right.

Again, it is easily checked that this equation is well-posed with
h\in C_{\operatorname{loc}}([s,\infty);\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2}))
whenever
h_{s}\in\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})
.
Our proof of Proposition
7.1
is based on the following three preliminary lemmas, the proofs of which are postponed to Sections
7.3
,
7.4
,
7.5
,
and
7.6

below.

Lemma 7.2

(Exponential decay on restricted space)
.

Let
\kappa_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
(i) and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
.
There exists
\lambda_{1}>0
(only depending on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
) such that the following holds: for all
k\geq 0
,
h_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,
t\geq s\geq 0
, and
\delta\in[0,1]
,

\Big\|V_{t,s}h_{s}-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}h_{s}\Big\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\\
\,\leq\,C_{k}e^{-\delta\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\Big(1+\kappa\big(e^{-\lambda_{1}s}Q(\mu_{\circ})\big)^{\delta}\Big)\Big(1+e^{-\lambda_{1}s}\big(e^{\lambda_{1}t}\wedge Q(\mu_{\circ})\big)\Big)^{\kappa^{2}C_{k}}\\
\,\leq\,C_{k}e^{-\delta\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\Big(1+e^{-\lambda_{1}s}Q(\mu_{\circ})\Big)^{\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{k}},

for some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

Lemma 7.3

(Exponential decay for modified operator)
.

Let
\kappa_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
(i) and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
.
There exists
\lambda_{2}>0
(only depending on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
), such that the following holds: given
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq 1
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,a
), choosing
\Lambda,R
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,p,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
), we have for all
k\geq 0
,
h_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq s\geq 0
,

(7.6)

\displaystyle\|W_{t,s}h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a}

\displaystyle e^{-p\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})},

(7.7)

\displaystyle\|W_{t,s}h_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a}

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|h_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})},

where the multiplicative constants only depend on
d,\beta,k,p,q,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

Lemma 7.4

(Regularization estimate)
.

Let
\kappa_{0},\lambda_{2}
be as in Theorem
2.13
(i) and Lemma
7.3
, respectively, and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
.
There is some
n\geq 1
large enough (only depending on
d
) such that the following holds: given
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq 1
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,a
), choosing
\Lambda,R
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,p,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
),
we have for all
k\geq 0
,
h_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq s\geq 0
,

\int_{s\leq s_{1}\leq\ldots\leq s_{n}\leq t}\|\mathcal{A}W_{t,s_{n}}\mathcal{A}W_{s_{n},s_{n-1}}\ldots\mathcal{A}W_{s_{1},s}h_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}s_{n}\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a}\,e^{-p\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})},

where the multiplicative constant only depends on
d,\beta,k,p,q,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+d+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

With those lemmas at hand, we are now in position to conclude the proof of Proposition
7.1
based on the enlargement theory of Gualdani, Mischler, and Mouhot
[
57
,
80
]
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Let
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
be defined in Lemmas
7.2
and
7.3
, respectively,
and let
1<q\leq 2
,
k\geq 0
, and
0<p\leq 1
be fixed with
pq^{\prime}\gg 1
large enough in the sense of Lemmas
7.3
and
7.4
.
We note that the space
H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})
is continuously embedded in
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
: by definition of dual norms, we find for all
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,

(7.8)

\|h\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}\,\|h\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})},

where the constant only depends on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
In this setting, we can appeal to enlargement theory to extend the estimates of Lemma
7.2
to
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
:
by Lemmas
7.2
,
7.3
, and
7.4
, we can apply
[
80
, Theorem 1.1]
and the conclusion precisely follows.
For completeness, we include a short proof as the present situation does not exactly fit in the semigroup setting of
[
80
]
.
Starting point is the following form of the Duhamel formula: based on the decomposition (
7.5
), the fundamental solution operators
\{V_{t,s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t}
can be expanded around
\{W_{t,s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t}
via

V_{t,s}\,=\,W_{t,s}+\int_{s}^{t}V_{t,u}\mathcal{A}W_{u,s}\,\mathrm{d}u.

By iteration, we get for any
n\geq 1
,

V_{t,s}\,=\,W_{t,s}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\int_{s}^{t}W_{t,u}(\mathcal{A}W)^{j}_{u,s}\,\mathrm{d}u+\int_{s}^{t}V_{t,u}(\mathcal{A}W)^{n}_{u,s}\,\mathrm{d}u,

where we have set for abbreviation, for all
j\geq 1
and
0\leq s_{0}\leq s_{j}
,

(\mathcal{A}W)^{j}_{s_{j},s_{0}}\,:=\,\int_{s_{0}\leq s_{1}\leq\ldots\leq s_{j-1}\leq s_{j}}\mathcal{A}W_{s_{j},s_{j-1}}\mathcal{A}W_{s_{j-1},s_{j-2}}\ldots\mathcal{A}W_{s_{1},s_{0}}\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}s_{j-1}.

Given
h_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,
taking norms,
applying the exponential decay of Lemma
7.3
for
\{W_{t,s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t}
on the space
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
, noting that
A
is bounded on
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
and that
|\int_{\mathbb{X}}h|\lesssim_{k,p,q}\|h\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}
provided
pq^{\prime}>2d
, we get for any
n\geq 1
,

\Big\|V_{t,s}h_{s}-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}h_{s}\Big\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,=\,\Big\|V_{t,s}h_{s}-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}V_{t,s}h_{s}\Big\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,n,a}\,\big(1+(t-s)^{n}\big)e^{-p\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\\
+\int_{s}^{t}\Big\|V_{t,u}(\mathcal{A}W)^{n}_{u,s}h_{s}-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}(\mathcal{A}W)^{n}_{u,s}h_{s}\Big\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\mathrm{d}u.

In order to estimate the last right-hand side term, we recall the embedding (
7.8
) and we use the exponential relaxation of Lemma
7.2
for
\{V_{t,s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t}
on the space
H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})
: for any
\delta\in[0,1]
, we obtain

\Big\|V_{t,s}h_{s}-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}h_{s}\Big\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,n,a}\,\big(1+(t-s)^{n}\big)e^{-p\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\\
+\Big(1+e^{-\lambda_{1}s}Q(\mu_{\circ})\Big)^{\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{k}}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\delta\lambda_{1}(t-u)}\|(\mathcal{A}W)^{n}_{u,s}h_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\mathrm{d}u,

for some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
Now using the regularization estimate of Lemma
7.4
for
n\geq 1
large enough (only depending on
d
), we are led to

\Big\|V_{t,s}h_{s}-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}h_{s}\Big\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a}\,\big(1+(t-s)^{n}\big)e^{-p\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\\
+\|h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\Big(1+e^{-\lambda_{1}s}Q(\mu_{\circ})\Big)^{\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{k}}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\delta\lambda_{1}(t-u)}e^{-p\lambda_{2}(u-s)}\,\mathrm{d}u,

and the conclusion follows (up to renaming
\frac{1}{2}(\lambda_{1}\wedge\lambda_{2})
as
\lambda_{1}
).
∎

7.2.
Proof of Theorem
2.13
(ii)

In this section, we establish Theorem
2.13
(ii) perturbatively as a consequence of Proposition
7.1
.
As a preliminary, we start with the following a priori estimates for the mean-field evolution (
1.28
) on the weighted spaces
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\kappa\in[0,1]
. There exists
\lambda>0
(only depending on
\beta,a
) such that the following holds: given
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq 1
with
pq^{\prime}\geq 4d
, we have for all
k\geq 1
and
t\geq 0
,

\|m(t,\mu_{\circ})\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\langle z\rangle^{p}\,m(t,\mu_{\circ})\,\lesssim\,1+e^{-p\lambda t}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{p},

where multiplicative constants only depend on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

Proof.

Set
\mu_{t}:=m(t,\mu_{\circ})
for abbreviation.
Noting that for
q^{\prime}\geq pq^{\prime}\geq 4d
and
k\geq 1
the Sobolev embedding yields

\|\mu_{t}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\leq\,\|\mu_{t}\|_{W^{-1,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\langle z\rangle^{p}\,\mu_{t}(\mathrm{d}z)\,\leq\,\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\langle z\rangle^{2}\mu_{t}(\mathrm{d}z)\Big)^{\frac{p}{2}}\,=\,Q(\mu_{t})^{p},

the conclusion follows from Lemma
2.12
(i).
∎

With this estimate at hand, we can now conclude the proof of Theorem
2.13
(ii) in the Langevin setting. As we treat the (compact) convolution term
(L_{\mu}-R_{\mu})h=\kappa(\nabla W\ast h)\cdot\nabla_{v}\mu
perturbatively, we emphasize that some care is needed to obtain a result that is valid for
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
independently of
k,p,q
. This is achieved through an iterative procedure.

Proof of Theorem
2.13
(ii).

By a standard approximation argument, it suffices to consider
f_{\circ}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})

with
\int_{\mathbb{X}}f_{\circ}=0
.
In that case, the well-posedness of the Cauchy problem (
ii
) is standard and it remains to establish the stability estimate (
2.24
).
Let
\kappa_{0},\lambda_{1}
be as in Proposition
7.1
, and assume that
\lambda_{1}
is chosen to be smaller than the exponent
\lambda
in Lemma
7.5
.
In terms of the modified linearized operator
R_{\mu}
defined in (
7.1
), further setting
\mu_{t}:=m(t,\mu_{\circ})
for abbreviation,
and defining the operator

A_{t}h\,:=\,(\nabla W\ast h)\cdot\nabla_{v}\mu_{t},

the equation (
ii
)
can be reformulated as

\partial_{t}f_{t}\,=\,R_{\mu_{t}}f_{t}+\kappa A_{t}f_{t},

hence, by Duhamel’s formula,

f_{t}=V_{t,0}f_{\circ}+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}V_{t,s}A_{s}f_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Iterating this formula,
we get for any
n\geq 1
,

(7.9)

f_{t}\,=\,V_{t,0}f_{\circ}+\sum_{m=2}^{n}\kappa^{m-1}\int_{0\leq s_{1}\leq\ldots\leq s_{m-1}\leq t}V_{t,s_{m-1}}A_{s_{m-1}}V_{s_{m-1},s_{m-2}}\ldots A_{s_{1}}V_{s_{1},0}f_{\circ}\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}s_{m-1}\\
+\kappa^{n}\int_{0\leq s_{1}\leq\ldots\leq s_{n}\leq t}V_{t,s_{n}}A_{s_{n}}V_{s_{n},s_{n-1}}\ldots A_{s_{2}}V_{s_{2},s_{1}}A_{s_{1}}f_{s_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}s_{n}.

From here, we split the proof into two steps.

Step
1. Estimate on
A_{t}
:
given
1<q_{0},q\leq 2
and
0<p_{0},p\leq 1
with
p_{0}q_{0}^{\prime},pq^{\prime}\geq 4d
, we have for all
k_{0}\geq 2
,
k\geq 0
,
h\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq 0
,

(7.10)

\|A_{t}h\|_{W^{-k_{0},q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}\,\Big(1+e^{-p_{0}\lambda_{1}t}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{p_{0}}\Big)\|h\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})},

where the multiplicative constant only depends on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

By definition of
A_{t}
, given
1<q_{0},q\leq 2
and
0<p_{0},p\leq 1
with
pq^{\prime}\geq 4d
, we can bound for all
k_{0}\geq 2
,
k\geq 0
,
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|A_{t}h\|_{W^{-k_{0},q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(\nabla W\ast h)\mu_{t}\|_{W^{-1,q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mu_{t}\|_{W^{-1,q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})}\|W\ast h\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k}

\displaystyle\|\mu_{t}\|_{W^{-1,q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})}\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|h\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}.

Now applying Lemma
7.5
, provided that
p_{0}q_{0}^{\prime}\geq 4d
and recalling
\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}
, the claim follows.

Step
2. Conclusion.

Starting from (
7.9
), leaving the last term aside, appealing to (
7.10
) with
k_{0}=k
,
q_{0}=q
,
p_{0}=p
, as well as to the ergodic estimates of Proposition
7.1
, we obtain for all
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq 1
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,a
),
k\geq 2
,
n\geq 1
,
t\geq 0
, and
\delta\in[0,1]
,

(7.11)

\|f_{t}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\leq\,C_{k,p,q}e^{-(\delta\wedge p)\lambda_{1}t}\|f_{\circ}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\tfrac{(\kappa C_{k,p,q}t)^{m}}{m!}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{m(p+\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{k})}\\
+\kappa^{n}\int_{0\leq s_{1}\leq\ldots\leq s_{n}\leq t}\|V_{t,s_{n}}A_{s_{n}}V_{s_{n},s_{n-1}}\ldots A_{s_{2}}V_{s_{2},s_{1}}A_{s_{1}}f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\mathrm{d}s_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}s_{n},

for some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
,
and some constant
C_{k,p,q}
further depending on
p,q
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+d+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
It remains to estimate the last right-hand side term, for which more care is needed in order to avoid a bad dependency in the multiplicative constant.
For that purpose, given a reference exponent
0<p_{0}\leq 1
, first note that we can choose
1<q_{0}\leq 2
with
p_{0}q_{0}^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,a
), such that:

—

both Lemma
7.5
and Proposition
7.1
hold in the space
W^{-2,q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})
;

—

for all
k\geq 2
,
1<q\leq q_{0}
, and
0<p\leq\frac{1}{2}p_{0}
, we have the following embedding, for all
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,

\|h\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\lesssim\,\|h\|_{W^{-2,q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})},

where the multiplicative constant only depends on
d
.

With this choice, for all
k\geq 2
,
1<q\leq q_{0}
,
0<p\leq\frac{1}{2}p_{0}
, and
\delta\in[0,1]
, applying the above embedding and then using iteratively Proposition
7.1
in
W^{-2,q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})
and the result (
7.10
) of Step 1 with
k=k_{0}=2
,
q=q_{0}
, and
p=p_{0}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\|V_{t,s_{n}}A_{s_{n}}V_{s_{n},s_{n-1}}\ldots A_{s_{2}}V_{s_{2},s_{1}}A_{s_{1}}f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|V_{t,s_{n}}A_{s_{n}}V_{s_{n},s_{n-1}}\ldots A_{s_{2}}V_{s_{2},s_{1}}A_{s_{1}}f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-2,q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{n}\|A_{s_{1}}f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-2,q_{0}}(\langle z\rangle^{p_{0}})}e^{-(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}(t-s_{1})}\Big(1+e^{-\delta\lambda_{1}s_{1}}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\prod_{j=2}^{n}\Big(1+e^{-(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}s_{j}}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{p_{0}+\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{0}}\Big),

for some constant
C_{0}
only depending on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, and some constant
C_{1}
further depending on
p_{0},q_{0},\|W\|_{W^{d+3,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
Now applying (
7.10
) with
k_{0}=2
, given
1<q\leq q_{0}
and
0<p\leq\frac{1}{2}p_{0}
with
pq^{\prime}\geq 4d
, we deduce for all
k\geq 2
and
\delta\in[0,1]
,

\|V_{t,s_{n}}A_{s_{n}}V_{s_{n},s_{n-1}}\ldots A_{s_{2}}V_{s_{2},s_{1}}A_{s_{1}}f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\\
\,\leq\,C_{2}C_{1}^{n}\|f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}e^{-(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}(t-s_{1})}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\Big(1+e^{-(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}s_{j}}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{p_{0}+\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{0}}\Big),

for some constant
C_{2}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
Expanding the product, we get

\|V_{t,s_{n}}A_{s_{n}}V_{s_{n},s_{n-1}}\ldots A_{s_{2}}V_{s_{2},s_{1}}A_{s_{1}}f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\leq\,C_{2}C_{1}^{n}\|f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}e^{-(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}(t-s_{1})}\\
+C_{2}(2C_{1})^{n}\|f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{n(p_{0}+\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{0})}e^{-(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}(t-s_{1})}\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{-(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}s_{j}}.

Taking the time integral,
we are then led to

\int_{0\leq s_{1}\leq\ldots\leq s_{n}\leq t}\|V_{t,s_{n}}A_{s_{n}}V_{s_{n},s_{n-1}}\ldots A_{s_{2}}V_{s_{2},s_{1}}A_{s_{1}}f_{s_{1}}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\mathrm{d}s_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}s_{n}\\
\,\leq\,C_{2}(2C_{1})^{n}((\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1})^{1-n}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|f_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
+C_{2}(4C_{1})^{n}((\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1})^{1-n}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{n(p_{0}+\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{0})}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\delta\wedge p_{0})\lambda_{1}t}\|f_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\mathrm{d}s.

Inserting this into (
7.11
), applying Grönwall’s inequality, renaming the constants, and recalling that
q_{0}
can be chosen only depending on
d,\beta,a,p_{0}
, we conclude the following: given
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq\frac{1}{2}p_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2}
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,a
), we have for all
n\geq 1
,
k\geq 2
,
\delta\in[0,1]
, and
t\geq 0
,

\|f_{t}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\,\leq\,C_{k,p,q}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\delta\wedge p)\lambda_{1}t}\|f_{\circ}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\tfrac{(\kappa C_{k,p,q}t)^{m}}{m!}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{m(p+\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{k})}\\
\times\exp\bigg(C_{k}(\kappa\delta^{-1}C_{1})^{n}t+C_{k}(\kappa\delta^{-1}C_{1})^{n}Q(\mu_{\circ})^{n(p_{0}+\delta+\kappa^{2}C_{0})}\bigg),

for some constant
C_{0}
only depending on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{4,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, some constant
C_{1}
further depending on
p_{0}
,
\|W\|_{W^{d+3,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
,
some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, and some constant
C_{k,p,q}
further depending on
p,q
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+d+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
We can apply this last estimate for instance in the following two ways:

—

given
\theta\in(0,1]
, choosing
p_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\theta
,
\delta=\frac{1}{4}\theta
,
n=1
, and requiring
\kappa
to be small enough such that
\kappa\delta^{-1}C_{1}C_{k}\leq\frac{1}{4}(\delta\wedge p)\lambda_{1}
and
\kappa^{2}C_{0}\leq\frac{1}{4}\theta
;

—

choosing
p_{0}=\delta=1
, requiring
\kappa
to be small enough such that
\kappa\delta^{-1}C_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, and then choosing
n\geq 1
such that
2^{-n}C_{k}\leq\frac{1}{4}p\lambda_{1}
;

and the conclusion follows (with
\lambda_{0}:=\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{1}
).
∎

7.3.
Proof of Lemma
7.2
: ergodic estimates with Gibbs weight

This section is devoted to the proof of Lemma
7.2
. We start by considering the standard kinetic Fokker–Planck operator

R_{M}h\,:=\,\tfrac{1}{2}{\operatorname{div}}_{v}((\nabla_{v}+\beta v)h)-v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+(\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast M)\cdot\nabla_{v}h.

The exponential relaxation of the associated semigroup
\{e^{tR_{M}}\}_{t\geq 0}
on
\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})
was established in the seminal work of Dolbeault, Mouhot, and Schmeiser
[
41
]
based on hypocoercivity techniques.
We post-process this well-known result to further derive estimates on Sobolev spaces with arbitrary negative regularity. For that purpose, we appeal to a duality argument and argue by induction using parabolic estimates.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\kappa_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.13
(i) and let
\kappa\in[0,\kappa_{0}]
.
There exists
\lambda_{1}>0
(only depending on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
) such that for all
k\geq 0
,
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq 0
,

(7.12)

\Big\|e^{tR_{M}}h-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}h\Big\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-{1/2}})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}\,e^{-\lambda_{1}t}\|h\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-{1/2}})},

where the multiplicative factor only depends on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

Proof.

We set for abbreviation
\pi_{M}^{\bot}h:=h-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}h
, and we note that
\int_{\mathbb{X}}e^{tR_{M}}h=\int_{\mathbb{X}}h
for all
t\geq 0
.
By definition of dual norms, cf. (
7.3
), also recalling the definition of the steady state
M
, it suffices to show that there is some
\lambda_{1}>0
such that for all
k\geq 0
,
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq 0
,

\|\pi_{M}^{\bot}e^{tR_{M}^{*}}h\|_{H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}\,e^{-\lambda_{1}t}\|h\|_{H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})},

where
R_{M}^{*}
stands for the dual Fokker–Planck operator

(7.13)

R_{M}^{*}h\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}{\operatorname{div}}_{v}((\nabla_{v}+\beta v)h)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h-(\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast M)\cdot\nabla_{v}h.

We shall actually prove the following more detailed estimate, further capturing the dissipation: there is some
\lambda_{1}>0
such that for all
k\geq 0
,
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq 0
,

(7.14)

e^{\lambda_{1}t}\|\pi_{M}^{\bot}e^{tR_{M}^{*}}h\|_{H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})}+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda_{1}s}\|(\nabla_{v}+\beta v)e^{sR_{M}^{*}}h\|_{H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}\,\|h\|_{H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})}.

We split the proof into two steps.

Step
1. Case
k=0
: there is some
\lambda_{1}>0
(only depending on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{X})}
) such that for all
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
and
t\geq 0
,

(7.15)

\|\pi_{M}^{\bot}e^{tR_{M}^{*}}h\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a}\,e^{-\lambda_{1}t}\|\pi_{M}^{\bot}h\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}.

This was precisely established by Dolbeault, Mouhot, and Schmeiser in
[
41
, Theorem 10]
.

Step
2. Conclusion: proof of (
7.14
).

Given
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, we set for shortness
J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}:=\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}\pi_{M}^{\bot}e^{tR_{M}^{*}}h
for multi-indices
\alpha,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
.
By definition, it satisfies

(7.16)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}=R_{M}^{*}J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}+r_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma},\quad\text{for $t\geq 0$},\\
J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}|_{t=0}=\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}(\pi_{M}^{\bot}h),\end{array}\right.

where the source term
r_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}
is given by

(7.17)

r_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\,:=\,[\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma},R_{M}^{*}]\pi_{M}^{\bot}e^{tR_{M}^{*}}h.

On the one hand, by Duhamel’s formula in form of

J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\,=\,e^{tR_{M}^{*}}\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}(\pi_{M}^{\bot}h)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)R_{M}^{*}}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}s,

the exponential decay (
7.15
) yields

(7.18)

\|J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a}\,e^{-\lambda_{1}t}\|\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}(\pi_{M}^{\bot}h)\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}\,\mathrm{d}s.

On the other hand, integrating by parts, the energy identity for equation (
7.16
) takes the form

(7.19)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{X}}J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}(R_{M}^{*}J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma})M^{-1}+2\int_{\mathbb{X}}J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}r_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}M^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\|(\nabla_{v}+\beta v)J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+2\|J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}\|r_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}.

Regarding the dissipation term in this last estimate, we make the following observation: integrating by parts and using
\nabla_{v}M^{-1}=\beta vM^{-1}
, we find for all
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,

(7.20)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}f|^{2}M^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{X}}M^{-1}f\big((\nabla_{v}+\beta v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{X}}M^{-1}f\,{\operatorname{div}}_{v}\big((\nabla_{v}+\beta v)f\big)+\beta d\int_{\mathbb{X}}|f|^{2}M^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}|(\nabla_{v}+\beta v)f|^{2}M^{-1}+\beta d\int_{\mathbb{X}}|f|^{2}M^{-1}.

Using this to replace half of the dissipation term in (
7.19
), we get

\partial_{t}\|J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(\|\nabla_{v}J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\|(\nabla_{v}+\beta v)J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)\\
\,\leq\,\tfrac{\beta d}{2}\|J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+2\|J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}\|r_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})},

and thus, by Grönwall’s inequality, for all
\lambda\geq 0
,

\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Big(e^{2\lambda s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}\Big(\|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\|(\nabla_{v}+\beta v)J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\,\lesssim_{\beta,d,\lambda}\,\|\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}(\pi_{M}^{\bot}h)\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}e^{\lambda s}\|r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

where the multiplicative constant is of the form
C_{d,\beta}(1+\lambda)
.
Now using (
7.18
) to bound the last term, we obtain for all
0\leq\lambda<\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda_{1}
,

\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Big(e^{2\lambda s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}\Big(\|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\|(\nabla_{v}+\beta v)J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},a}\,\|\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}(\pi_{M}^{\bot}h)\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}e^{\lambda^{\prime}s}\|r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}.

By definition of
R_{M}^{*}
, cf. (
7.13
), the source term
r^{\alpha,\gamma}
defined in (
7.17
) takes the form

r_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}=\sum_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\binom{\gamma}{e_{i}}J_{t}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}+\!\!\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\binom{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}\binom{\gamma}{\gamma^{\prime}}\nabla_{x}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}\big(\tfrac{\beta}{2}v-\nabla A-\kappa\nabla W\ast M\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}J_{t}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}},

so the above yields for all
0\leq\lambda<\lambda^{\prime}<\lambda_{1}
,

\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Big(e^{2\lambda s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)+\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}\Big(\|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\|(\nabla_{v}+\beta v)J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},\alpha,\gamma,a}\,\|\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}(\pi_{M}^{\bot}h)\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\Big(e^{2\lambda^{\prime}s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)\\
+\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda^{\prime}s}\|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

A direct induction then yields for all
\alpha,\gamma\geq 0
,
\lambda\in[0,\lambda_{1})
, and
t\geq 0
,

e^{2\lambda t}\|J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}\Big(\|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}+\|(\nabla_{v}+\beta v)J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\lambda,\alpha,\gamma,a}\,\|\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}(\pi_{M}^{\bot}h)\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-{1/2}})}^{2}.

Recalling
J_{t}^{\alpha,\gamma}=\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}\pi_{M}^{\bot}e^{tR_{M}^{*}}h
and
\pi_{M}^{\bot}h=h-M\int_{\mathbb{X}}h
, this proves the claim (
7.14
) (up to a redefinition of
\lambda_{1}
).
∎

Starting from the above exponential decay for the Fokker–Planck semigroup, we can conclude the proof of Lemma
7.2
by means of a perturbation argument.

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

Let
\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}
be as in Theorem
2.13
(i) and in Lemma
7.6
, respectively.
We split the proof into three steps.

Step
1. Perturbative semigroup estimate: proof that for all
k\geq 0
,
g_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq s\geq 0
,

(7.21)

\|V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\leq\,C_{k}e^{-(\lambda_{1}-\kappa^{2}C_{k})(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})},

for some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

By Duhamel’s formula,
