V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}\,=\,e^{(t-s)R_{M}}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}+\kappa\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)R_{M}}\big((\nabla W\ast(\mu_{u}-M))\cdot\nabla_{v}(V_{u,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s})\big)\,\mathrm{d}u,

and thus, for all
h_{t}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, integrating by parts and using
\pi_{M}^{\bot}e^{tR_{M}}=e^{tR_{M}}\pi_{M}^{\bot}
,

\int_{\mathbb{X}}(V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s})h_{t}M^{-1}\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big(e^{(t-s)R_{M}^{*}}\pi_{M}^{\bot}h_{t}\big)g_{s}M^{-1}\\
-\kappa\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\big((\nabla_{v}+\beta v)e^{(t-u)R_{M}^{*}}h_{t}\big)\cdot(\nabla W\ast(\mu_{u}-M))(V_{u,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s})M^{-1}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}u.

Applying the exponential decay estimate (
7.14
) of Lemma
7.6
, and taking the supremum over
h_{t}
in
H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})
, we deduce for all
k\geq 0
and
t\geq s\geq 0
,

(7.22)

\|V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}\,e^{-\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\\
+\kappa\Big(\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2\lambda_{1}(t-u)}\|V_{u,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}\|\nabla W\ast(\mu_{u}-M)\|_{W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Simply bounding
\|\nabla W\ast(\mu_{u}-M)\|_{W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 2\|W\|_{W^{k+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, this yields the claim (
7.21
) by Grönwall’s inequality.

Step
2. Improved estimate through control of moments: proof that for all
k\geq 0
,
g_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq s\geq 0
,

(7.23)

\|V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\leq\,C_{k}e^{-\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\Big(1+e^{-\lambda_{0}s}Q(\mu_{\circ})\Big)^{\kappa^{2}C_{k}},

for some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

Using Theorem
2.13
(i), we can estimate

\|\nabla W\ast(\mu_{t}-M)\|_{W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\leq\,\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{t},M)\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a}\,e^{-\lambda_{0}t}\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{\circ},M)\,\lesssim_{\beta}\,e^{-\lambda_{0}t}Q(\mu_{\circ}),

and thus, combining this with the trivial bound
\|\nabla W\ast(\mu_{t}-M)\|_{W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 2\|W\|_{W^{k+1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, we get by interpolation, for any
\delta\in[0,1]
,

(7.24)

\|\nabla W\ast(\mu_{t}-M)\|_{W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}\,\big(e^{-\lambda_{0}t}Q(\mu_{\circ})\big)^{\delta},

where the multiplicative constant only depends on
d,\beta,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
Rather using this in the bound (
7.22
) of Step 1, we get by Grönwall’s inequality, for any
\delta\in[0,1]
,

\|V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\leq\,C_{k}e^{-\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\exp\Big(\kappa^{2}C_{k}\frac{(e^{-\lambda_{0}s}Q(\mu_{\circ}))^{2\delta}}{2\delta}\Big),

for some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
. Choosing
2\delta=\log(e+e^{-\lambda_{0}s}Q(\mu_{\circ}))^{-1}
, which indeed belongs to
(0,1)
, this entails

\|V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\leq\,C_{k}e^{-\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\exp\Big(\kappa^{2}C_{k}\log\big(e+e^{-\lambda_{0}s}Q(\mu_{\circ})\big)\Big)\\
\,=\,C_{k}e^{-\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\Big(e+e^{-\lambda_{0}s}Q(\mu_{\circ})\Big)^{\kappa^{2}C_{k}},

which proves the claim (
7.23
).

Step
3. Conclusion.

First note that the results of Steps 1 and 2 can be unified as follows: for all
k\geq 0
,
g_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, and
t\geq s\geq 0
, we have

(7.25)

\|V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\leq\,C_{k}e^{-\lambda_{1}(t-s)}\|g_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\Big(1+e^{-\lambda_{0}s}\big(e^{\lambda_{0}t}\wedge Q(\mu_{\circ})\big)\Big)^{\kappa^{2}C_{k}},

for some constant
C_{k}
only depending on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
It remains to replace
V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}
by
\pi_{M}^{\bot}V_{t,s}
in this estimate.
For that purpose, let us decompose

(7.26)

\pi_{M}^{\bot}V_{t,s}g_{s}\,=\,V_{t,s}\pi_{M}^{\bot}g_{s}+\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}g_{s}\Big)r_{t,s},\qquad r_{t,s}\,:=\,\pi_{M}^{\bot}V_{t,s}M.

By definition,
r_{t,s}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{t,s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{M}V_{t,s}M+\kappa(\nabla W\ast(\mu_{t}-M))\cdot\nabla_{v}V_{t,s}M

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{M}r_{t,s}+\kappa(\nabla W\ast(\mu_{t}-M))\cdot\nabla_{v}r_{t,s}+\kappa(\nabla W\ast(\mu_{t}-M))\cdot\nabla_{v}M,

with
r_{t,s}|_{t=s}=0
. By Duhamel’s formula, this yields

\displaystyle r_{t,s}\,=\,\kappa\int_{s}^{t}V_{t,u}\big(\nabla W\ast(\mu_{u}-M)\cdot\nabla_{v}M\big)\,\mathrm{d}u.

Now applying (
7.25
) together with (
7.24
), we get for all
k\geq 0
,
t\geq s\geq 0
, and
\delta\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\|r_{t,s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}

\displaystyle\kappa\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\lambda_{1}(t-u)}\|\nabla W\ast(\mu_{u}-M)\|_{W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big(1+e^{-\lambda_{0}u}\big(e^{\lambda_{0}t}\wedge Q(\mu_{\circ})\big)\Big)^{\kappa^{2}C_{k}}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}

\displaystyle\kappa Q(\mu_{\circ})^{\delta}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\delta(\lambda_{0}\wedge\lambda_{1})t}\Big(1+e^{-\lambda_{0}s}\big(e^{\lambda_{0}t}\wedge Q(\mu_{\circ})\big)\Big)^{\kappa^{2}C_{k}}.

Combined with (
7.25
) and (
7.26
), this yields the conclusion up to redefining
\lambda_{1}
.
∎

7.4.
Proof of Lemma
7.3
on
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})

This section is devoted to the proof of (
7.6
).
Instead of
\langle z\rangle^{p}
, we shall consider deformed weights of the form
\omega^{p}
in terms of

(7.27)

\omega(x,v)\,:=\,1+\beta\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}|v|^{2}+a|x|^{2}+\eta x\cdot v\Big),

where the parameter
0<\eta\ll 1
will be properly chosen later on. We naturally restrict to
\eta\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\sqrt{a}
, which ensures

\omega(x,v)\simeq_{\beta,a}\langle z\rangle^{2}.

Note that those weights differ from the choice used in
[
80
]
and are critical for the improved result we establish in this work.
We define the weighted negative Sobolev spaces
W^{-k,q}(\omega^{p})
exactly as the spaces
W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
in (
2.20
), simply replacing the weight
\langle z\rangle^{p}
by
\omega^{p}
in the definition.
Comparing
\omega
and
\langle z\rangle^{2}
, the definition of dual norms easily ensures for all
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,

(7.28)

\|h\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{2p})}\,\simeq_{\beta,k,p,a}\,\|h\|_{W^{-k,q}(\omega^{p})}.

For a densely-defined operator
X
on
\operatorname{L}^{q}(\omega^{p}):=W^{0,q}(\omega^{p})
, we denote by
X^{*,p}
its adjoint on
\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})
with respect to the weighted duality product
(g,h)\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{X}}gh\omega^{p}
: more precisely,
X^{*,p}
stands for the closed operator on
\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})
defined by the relation

\int_{\mathbb{X}}g(Xh)\omega^{p}\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}h(X^{*,p}g)\omega^{p}.

In particular, for
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{X})
, we consider the weighted adjoint
B_{\mu}^{*,p}
of
B_{\mu}
, which takes the explicit form

(7.29)

B_{\mu}^{*,p}h\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\triangle_{v}h+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h-\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta v+\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu-\omega^{-p}\nabla_{v}\omega^{p}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h\\
+\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\omega^{-p}\triangle_{v}\omega^{p}+\omega^{-p}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\omega^{p}-\omega^{-p}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta v+\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\omega^{p}-\Lambda\chi_{R}\Big)h.

By the equivalence of norms (
7.28
) and by the definition of dual norms, it suffices to prove that there is some
0<\eta\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{a}
and some
\lambda_{2}>0
(only depending on
d,\beta,a
) such that the following result holds:
given
1<q\leq 2
and
0<p\leq 1
with
pq^{\prime}\gg_{\beta,a}1
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,a
),
choosing
\Lambda,R
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,p,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
), if
g\in C([0,t];\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X}))
satisfies the backward Cauchy problem

(7.30)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{s}g_{s}=-B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,p}g_{s},\quad\text{for $0\leq s\leq t$},\\
g_{s}|_{s=t}=g_{t},\end{array}\right.

for some
t\geq 0
and
g_{t}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,
then we have for all
k\geq 0
and
0\leq s\leq t
,

(7.31)

\|g_{s}\|_{W^{k,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a}\,e^{-\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|g_{t}\|_{W^{k,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}.

We split the proof into two steps, starting with the case
k=0
before treating all
k\geq 0
by induction.

Step
1. Proof of (
7.31
) for
k=0
.

By definition of
B_{\mu}^{*,p}
and by integration by parts, we find

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,=\,-q^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}-2}g_{s}B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,p}g_{s}\\
\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}q^{\prime}(q^{\prime}-1)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}-2}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}}\Big(q^{\prime}\omega^{-p}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\omega^{p}-q^{\prime}\omega^{-p}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta v+\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\omega^{p}\\
-q^{\prime}\Lambda\chi_{R}+\tfrac{1}{2}q^{\prime}\omega^{-p}\triangle_{v}\omega^{p}+\tfrac{\beta d}{2}-{\operatorname{div}}_{v}(\omega^{-p}\nabla_{v}\omega^{p})\Big).

Now inserting the form of the weight
\omega
and explicitly computing its derivatives, we get after straightforward simplifications,

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{2}q^{\prime}(q^{\prime}-1)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}-2}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}\\
+\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}}\Big(pq^{\prime}\beta\big(\tfrac{\beta}{2}-\eta(1+\tfrac{1}{32a}\beta^{2})\big)\omega^{-1}|v|^{2}+2a\eta\beta pq^{\prime}\omega^{-1}|x|^{2}-\tfrac{\beta d}{2}+q^{\prime}\big(\Lambda\chi_{R}-C_{W,\beta,a}\omega^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\Big).

We show that we can choose our parameters in such a way that the last bracket be bounded below by a positive constant, which is the key to the desired exponential decay.
More precisely, choosing

\eta:=\min\big\{\tfrac{1}{4}\beta(1+\tfrac{1}{32a}\beta^{2})^{-1},\tfrac{1}{2}\sqrt{a}\big\},\qquad 4\lambda_{2}:=\min\{\tfrac{1}{2}\beta,2\eta\},

we get

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{2}q^{\prime}(q^{\prime}-1)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}-2}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}\\
+\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}}\Big(4pq^{\prime}\beta\lambda_{2}\omega^{-1}(\tfrac{1}{2}|v|^{2}+a|x|^{2})-\tfrac{\beta d}{2}+q^{\prime}\big(\Lambda\chi_{R}-C_{W,\beta,a}\omega^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\Big).

As the choice
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{a}
ensures

\tfrac{1}{2}|v|^{2}+a|x|^{2}\geq\tfrac{1}{2\beta}(\omega-1)
, this actually means

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{2}q^{\prime}(q^{\prime}-1)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}-2}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}}\Big(2pq^{\prime}\lambda_{2}-\tfrac{\beta d}{2}+q^{\prime}\big(\Lambda\chi_{R}-C_{W,\beta,a}\omega^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\Big).

Now, recalling the definition of the cut-off function
\chi_{R}
, we note that we can choose
\Lambda,R>0
large enough (only depending on
d,W,\beta,p,a
) such that

\Lambda\chi_{R}-C_{W,\beta,a}\omega^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\geq\,-\tfrac{1}{2}p\lambda_{2}.

Provided that
pq^{\prime}\geq 2\beta d\lambda_{2}^{-1}
,
we then obtain

(7.32)

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{2}q^{\prime}(q^{\prime}-1)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{q^{\prime}-2}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}+pq^{\prime}\lambda_{2}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}},

hence, by Grönwall’s inequality,

(7.33)

\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}\,\leq\,e^{-p\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|g_{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}.

that is, (
7.31
) for
k=0
.

Step
2. Proof of (
7.31
) for all
k\geq 0
.

For multi-indices
\alpha,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, we set
J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}:=\nabla^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla^{\gamma}_{v}g_{s}
.
Differentiating equation (
7.30
), we get

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{s}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}=-B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,p}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}-r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma},\quad\text{for $0\leq s\leq t$},\\
J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|_{s=t}=\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}g_{t},\end{array}\right.

where the remainder is given by

r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\,:=\,[\nabla^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla^{\gamma}_{v},B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,p}]g_{s}.

Repeating the proof of (
7.32
), for the choice of
\eta,\lambda_{2},\Lambda,R
in Step 1, we get

(7.34)

\partial_{s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{2}q^{\prime}(q^{\prime}-1)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{q^{\prime}-2}|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{2}+pq^{\prime}\lambda_{2}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}-q^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{q^{\prime}-2}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma},

and it remains to analyze the last contribution.
By definition of
B_{\mu}^{*,p}
, cf. (
7.29
), we can compute

(7.35)

\displaystyle r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\binom{\gamma}{e_{i}}J^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}_{s}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\binom{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}\binom{\gamma}{\gamma^{\prime}}\,{\operatorname{div}}_{v}\Big[J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\,\nabla_{x}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta v+\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu-\omega^{-p}\nabla_{v}\omega^{p}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\binom{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}\binom{\gamma}{\gamma^{\prime}}J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\,\nabla_{x}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\omega^{-p}\triangle_{v}\omega^{p}+\omega^{-p}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\omega^{p}-{\operatorname{div}}_{v}(\omega^{-p}\nabla_{v}\omega^{p})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\omega^{-p}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta v+\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\omega^{p}+\tfrac{\beta d}{2}-\Lambda\chi_{R}\Big).

Integrating by parts, this allows us to estimate

\int_{\mathbb{X}}|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{q^{\prime}-2}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,a}\,\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}-1}\Big(\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\|J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}+\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\|J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}\Big)\\
+q^{\prime}\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{q^{\prime}-2}|J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}||\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|.

Inserting this estimate into (
7.34
) and appealing to Young’s inequality to absorb
\nabla_{v}J^{\alpha,\gamma}
into the dissipation term, we are led to

\partial_{s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,\geq\,pq^{\prime}\lambda_{2}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}-(q^{\prime})^{2}C_{W,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,a}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}-2}\Big(\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\|J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\Big)\\
-q^{\prime}C_{W,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,a}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}-1}\Big(\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\|J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}+\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\|J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}\Big).

Further appealing to Young’s inequality, we get for all
\lambda<\lambda_{2}
,

\partial_{s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,\geq\,pq^{\prime}\lambda\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\\
-C_{W,\beta,\lambda,\alpha,\gamma,p,q,a}\Big(\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\|J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}+\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\|J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\Big),

and thus, by Grönwall’s inequality,

e^{pq^{\prime}\lambda(t-s)}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\lambda,\alpha,\gamma,p,q,a}\,\|\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}g_{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\\
+\int_{s}^{t}e^{pq^{\prime}\lambda(t-u)}\Big(\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\|J_{u}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}+\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\|J_{u}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}^{q^{\prime}}\Big)\mathrm{d}u.

Iterating this inequality and starting from the result (
7.33
) of Step 1 for
J^{0,0}_{s}=g_{s}
, the conclusion follows.
∎

7.5.
Proof of Lemma
7.3
on
H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})

This section is devoted to the proof of (
7.7
).
Taking inspiration from the work of Mischler and Mouhot
[
80
, Section 4.2]
, we consider deformed weights of the form
M^{-1}\zeta
with the factor
\zeta
given by

(7.36)

\zeta(x,v)\,:=\,1+\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{x\cdot v}{1+\frac{\eta}{2}|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}|v|^{2}}\Big)

where the parameter
\eta>0
will be properly chosen later on. Note that for any
\eta>0
we have

\tfrac{1}{2}\,\leq\,\zeta\,\leq\,\tfrac{3}{2}.

In these terms, we define the weighted negative Sobolev spaces
H^{-k}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})
exactly as
H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})
in (
7.3
), simply replacing the weight
M^{-1}
by
M^{-1}\zeta
in the definition. Comparing
M^{-1}\zeta
to
M^{-1}
, the definition of dual norms easily yields the equivalence, for all
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,

(7.37)

\|h\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\simeq_{k,\eta}\,\|h\|_{H^{-k}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}.

For a densely-defined operator
X
on
\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})
, we denote by
X^{*,\zeta}
its adjoint on
\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})
with respect to the weighted duality product
(g,h)\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{X}}ghM^{-1}\zeta
: more precisely,
X^{*,\zeta}
stands for the closed operator on
\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})
defined by the relation

\int_{\mathbb{X}}g(Xh)M^{-1}\zeta\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}h(X^{*,\zeta}h)M^{-1}\zeta.

By the equivalence of norms (
7.37
) and by definition of dual norms, it suffices to prove that there is some
0<\eta\leq 1
and
\lambda_{2}>0
(only depending on
d,\beta,a
, and
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
) such that the following result holds: choosing
\Lambda,R
large enough (only depending on
d,\beta,a
, and
\|W\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
), if
g\in C([0,t];\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2}))
satisfies the backward Cauchy problem

(7.38)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{s}g_{s}=-B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,\zeta}g_{s},\quad\text{for $0\leq s\leq t$},\\
g_{s}|_{s=t}=g_{t},\end{array}\right.

for some
t\geq 0
and
g_{t}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,
then we have for all
\lambda\in[0,\lambda_{2})
,
k\geq 0
, and
0\leq s\leq t
,

(7.39)

\|g_{s}\|_{H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\lambda,k,a}\,e^{-\lambda(t-s)}\|g_{t}\|_{H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})}.

We split the proof into two steps, starting with the case
k=0
before treating all
k\geq 0
by induction.

Step
1. Proof of (
7.39
) for
k=0
.

By definition of
B_{\mu}
and by integration by parts, we find

(7.40)

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}\,=\,-2\int_{\mathbb{X}}g_{s}(B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,\zeta}g_{s})M^{-1}\zeta\,=\,-2\int_{\mathbb{X}}g_{s}(B_{\mu_{s}}g_{s})M^{-1}\zeta\\
\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta}g_{s})|^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}M^{-1}\Big(\tfrac{1}{4}\zeta|\beta v|^{2}+(\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu_{s})\cdot\nabla_{v}\zeta-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\zeta\\
+2\Lambda\chi_{R}\zeta-\tfrac{\beta d}{2}\zeta+\kappa\beta\zeta v\cdot(\nabla W\ast(\mu_{s}-M))-|\nabla_{v}\sqrt{\zeta}|^{2}\Big).

We show that we can choose parameters in such a way that the last bracket be bounded below by a positive constant, which is the key to the desired exponential decay.
By definition of
\zeta
, cf. (
7.36
), and by Young’s inequality, we find

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\zeta|\beta v|^{2}+\nabla A\cdot\nabla_{v}\zeta-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\zeta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}|v|^{2}\bigg(\tfrac{1}{2}\zeta\beta^{2}-\frac{1}{1+\frac{\eta}{2}|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}|v|^{2}}\bigg)+a\frac{|x|^{2}}{1+\frac{\eta}{2}|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}|v|^{2}}-(a\eta^{-1}\!-\tfrac{1}{2}\eta)\frac{(x\cdot v)^{2}}{(1+\frac{\eta}{2}|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}|v|^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}|v|^{2}\bigg(\tfrac{1}{4}\beta^{2}-\frac{1+a\eta^{-2}}{1+\frac{\eta}{2}|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}|v|^{2}}\bigg)+\tfrac{1}{2}a\frac{|x|^{2}}{1+\frac{\eta}{2}|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}|v|^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}|v|^{2}\bigg(\tfrac{1}{4}\beta^{2}-\frac{1+2a\eta^{-2}}{1+\frac{\eta}{2}|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}|v|^{2}}\bigg)+a\eta^{-1}\bigg(1-\frac{1}{1+\frac{\eta}{2}|x|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta}|v|^{2}}\bigg).

Inserting this into (
7.40
), and further using
|\nabla_{v}\zeta|\lesssim\eta^{-1}\langle z\rangle^{-1}
, we obtain

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}\,\geq\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta}g_{s})|^{2}\\
+\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}M^{-1}\Big(a\eta^{-1}-C_{W,\beta,a}+\Lambda\chi_{R}+\tfrac{1}{10}|\beta v|^{2}\big(1-\eta^{-3}\langle z\rangle^{-2}C_{W,\beta,a}\big)-C_{W,\beta,a}\eta^{-2}\langle z\rangle^{-1}\Big).

Noting that the dissipation term can be bounded below as

\displaystyle|\nabla_{v}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta}g)|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta|^{2}|\nabla_{v}(\sqrt{M^{-1}}g)|^{2}-2|\nabla_{v}\zeta|^{2}|g|^{2}M^{-1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{8}|(\nabla_{v}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}v)g|^{2}M^{-1}-C\eta^{-2}\langle z\rangle^{-2}|g|^{2}M^{-1},

the above becomes

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{8}\|(\nabla_{v}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}v)g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}\\
+\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}M^{-1}\Big(a\eta^{-1}-C_{W,\beta,a}+\Lambda\chi_{R}+\tfrac{1}{10}|\beta v|^{2}\big(1-\eta^{-3}\langle z\rangle^{-2}C_{W,\beta,a}\big)-C_{W,\beta,a}\eta^{-2}\langle z\rangle^{-1}\Big).

Now let us choose

\eta:=\tfrac{1}{2}aC_{W,\beta,a}^{-1}
,
and note that, by definition of the cut-off function
\chi_{R}
, we may then choose
\Lambda,R>0
large enough (only depending on
d,W,\beta,a
) such that

\Lambda\chi_{R}+\tfrac{1}{10}|\beta v|^{2}\big(1-\eta^{-3}\langle z\rangle^{-2}C_{W,\beta,a}\big)-C_{W,\beta,a}\eta^{-2}\langle z\rangle^{-1}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{20}|\beta v|^{2}-\tfrac{1}{4}a\eta^{-1}.

Further setting
\lambda_{2}:=\tfrac{1}{12}a\eta^{-1}
, this choice leads us to

(7.41)

\partial_{s}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{8}\|(\nabla_{v}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}v)g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{20}\|\beta vg_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}+2\lambda_{2}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}.

In particular, by Grönwall’s inequality,

(7.42)

\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}\,\lesssim\,e^{-\lambda_{2}(t-s)}\|g_{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})},

that is, (
7.39
) for
k=0
.

Step
2. Proof of (
7.39
) for all
k
.

For multi-indices
\alpha,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, we set
J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}:=\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}g_{s}
. Differentiating equation (
7.38
), we get

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{s}J^{\alpha,\gamma}_{s}=-B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,\zeta}J^{\alpha,\gamma}_{s}-r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma},\quad\text{for $0\leq s\leq t$},\\[2.84526pt]
J^{\alpha,\gamma}_{s}|_{s=t}=\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}g_{t},\end{array}\right.

where the remainder is given by

r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\,:=\,[\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma},B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,\zeta}]g_{s}.

Repeating the proof of (
7.41
), for the choice of
\eta,\lambda_{2},\Lambda,R
in Step 1, we get

(7.43)

\partial_{s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{8}\|(\nabla_{v}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}v)J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{20}\|\beta vJ_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}+2\lambda_{2}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}\\
-2\int_{\mathbb{X}}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}M^{-1}\zeta.

and it remains to analyze the last contribution.
By definition of
B_{\mu}
, the weighted adjoint
B_{\mu}^{*,\zeta}
takes the explicit form

B_{\mu}^{*,\zeta}h\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\triangle_{v}h+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+\Big(\tfrac{\beta}{2}v-\nabla A-\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu+\tfrac{1}{\zeta}\nabla_{v}\zeta\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h\\
+\Big(\tfrac{\beta d}{2}+\tfrac{1}{2\zeta}\triangle_{v}\zeta-\kappa\beta v\cdot(\nabla W\ast(\mu-M))\\
+\tfrac{1}{\zeta}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\zeta+\tfrac{1}{\zeta}\big(\tfrac{\beta}{2}v-\nabla A-\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\zeta-\Lambda\chi_{R}\Big)h,

and we may then compute

r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\,=\,[\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma},B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,\zeta}]g_{s}\,=\,\sum_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\binom{\gamma}{e_{i}}J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\\
+\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\binom{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}\binom{\gamma}{\gamma^{\prime}}\nabla_{x}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}\Big(\tfrac{\beta}{2}v-\nabla A-\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu+\tfrac{1}{\zeta}\nabla_{v}\zeta\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}J^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}_{s}\\
+\sum_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\binom{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}\binom{\gamma}{\gamma^{\prime}}J^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}_{s}\,\nabla_{x}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}\Big(\tfrac{\beta d}{2}+\tfrac{1}{2\zeta}\triangle_{v}\zeta-\kappa\beta v\cdot(\nabla W\ast(\mu-M))\\
+\tfrac{1}{\zeta}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\zeta+\tfrac{1}{\zeta}\big(\tfrac{\beta}{2}v-\nabla A-\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\zeta-\Lambda\chi_{R}\Big),

from which we easily estimate

\int_{\mathbb{X}}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}M^{-1}\zeta\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,a}\,\Big(\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}+\|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}+\|\beta vJ_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}\Big)\\
\times\Big(\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\|J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}+\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\|J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}\Big).

Inserting this into (
7.43
), and appealing to Young’s inequality to absorb
J^{\alpha,\gamma}
,
\nabla_{v}J^{\alpha,\gamma}
, and
\beta vJ^{\alpha,\gamma}
into the dissipation terms, we deduce for all
\lambda<\lambda_{2}
,

\partial_{s}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}\,\geq\,2\lambda\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\sqrt{M^{-1}\zeta})}^{2}\\
-C_{W,\beta,\lambda,\alpha,\gamma,a}\Big(\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\|J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}+\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\|J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}\Big),

and thus, by Grönwall’s inequality,

e^{2\lambda(t-s)}\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\lambda,\alpha,\gamma,a}\,\|\nabla^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}g_{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}\\
+\int_{s}^{t}e^{2\lambda(t-u)}\Big(\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\|J_{u}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}+\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\|J_{u}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(M^{-1/2})}^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}u.

Iterating this inequality, and starting from the result (
7.42
) of Step 1 for
J^{0,0}_{s}=g_{s}
, the conclusion follows.
∎

7.6.
Proof of Lemma
7.4

In this section, we appeal again to duality but we shall use a slightly different notation than in Section
7.4
:
for a densely-defined operator
X
on
\operatorname{L}^{q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})
we now denote by
X^{*,p}
its adjoint on
\operatorname{L}^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})
with respect to the weighted duality product
(g,h)\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{X}}gh\langle z\rangle^{p}
. In other words, we use the same notation as in Section
7.4
for
X^{*,p}
, but now with the weight
\omega
replaced by
\langle z\rangle
.
We consider in particular the weighted adjoints
\{W_{t,s}^{*,p}\}_{t\geq s\geq 0}
of the fundamental operators
\{W_{t,s}\}_{t\geq s\geq 0}
, and we note that for all
t\geq 0
and
g_{t}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
the flow
g_{s}:=W_{t,s}^{*,p}g_{t}
is such that
g\in C([0,t];L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X}))
satisfies the backward Cauchy problem

(7.44)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{s}g_{s}=-B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,p}g_{s},\quad\text{for $0\leq s\leq t$},\\
g_{s}|_{s=t}=g_{t},\end{array}\right.

where
B_{\mu}^{*,p}
is the weighted adjoint of
B_{\mu}
. This operator
B_{\mu}^{*,p}
takes the explicit form (
7.29
) with
\omega
now replaced by
\langle z\rangle
.
The proof of Lemma
7.4
ultimately relies on the following result.

Lemma 7.7
.

For all
k\geq 0
,
0\leq p\leq 1
,
0\leq t-s\leq 1
, and
g_{t}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, we have

(7.45)

\big\|W^{*,p}_{t,s}g_{t}\big\|_{H^{k+1}(\mathbb{X})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,a}\,(t-s)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|g_{t}\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{X})},

where the constant only depends on
d,\beta,k,a
,
\|W\|_{W^{k+2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

We postpone the proof of this result for a moment and start by showing that Lemma
7.4
follows as a straightforward consequence.

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

We start by applying the interpolation argument of
[
80
, Lemma 2.4]
: thanks to the exponential decay estimates of Lemma
7.3
, it suffices to find some
\theta\geq 0
(only depending on
d
) such that for all
1<q\leq 2
,
k\geq 0
,
0<p\leq 1
,
0\leq t-s\leq 1
, and
h_{s}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
we have

\|\mathcal{A}W_{t,s}h_{s}\|_{H^{-k}(M^{-1/2})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a}\,(t-s)^{-\theta}\|h_{s}\|_{W^{-k,q}(\langle z\rangle^{p})}.

In order to prove this, we argue by duality:
more precisely, recalling
A=\Lambda\chi_{R}
, it suffices to find some
\theta\geq 0
such that for all
1<q\leq 2
,
k\geq 0
,
0<p\leq 1
,
0\leq t-s\leq 1
, and
g_{t}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,

(7.46)

\|W_{t,s}^{*,p}(\chi_{R}M^{-1}\langle z\rangle^{-p}g_{t})\|_{W^{k,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,k,p,q,a}\,(t-s)^{-\theta}\|g_{t}\|_{H^{k}(M^{-{1/2}})}.

By the Sobolev inequality with
2\leq q^{\prime}<\infty
, the left-hand side can be estimated as follows,

\|W_{t,s}^{*,p}(\chi_{R}M^{-1}\langle z\rangle^{-p}g_{t})\|_{W^{k,q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X})}\,\lesssim_{k,q}\,\|W_{t,s}^{*,p}(\chi_{R}M^{-1}\langle z\rangle^{-p}g_{t})\|_{H^{k+d}(\mathbb{X})},

and the desired bound (
7.46
) then follows with
\theta=\frac{3d}{2}
by iterating the result of Lemma
7.7
.
∎

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Lemma
7.7
. For
k=0
, this is in fact a standard consequence of the theory of hypoellipticity as in
[
64
,
95
,
80
]
. For
k>0
, we argue by induction, further using parabolic estimates similarly as in Section
7.4
.

Proof of Lemma
7.7
.

Given
t\geq 0
and
g_{t}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
, let
g_{s}:=W_{t,s}^{*,p}g_{t}
be the solution of the backward Cauchy problem (
7.44
), and recall that
B_{\mu}^{*,p}
takes the explicit form (
7.29
) with
\omega
replaced by
\langle z\rangle
,

B_{\mu}^{*,p}h\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\triangle_{v}h+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+A^{0}_{\mu,p}h-A^{1}_{\mu,p}\cdot\nabla_{v}h,

in terms of

\displaystyle A^{0}_{\mu,p}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\langle z\rangle^{-p}\triangle_{v}\langle z\rangle^{p}+\langle z\rangle^{-p}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\langle z\rangle^{p}-\langle z\rangle^{-p}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta v+\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\langle z\rangle^{p}-\Lambda\chi_{R},

\displaystyle A^{1}_{\mu,p}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\beta v+\nabla A+\kappa\nabla W\ast\mu-\langle z\rangle^{-p}\nabla_{v}\langle z\rangle^{p}.

We split the proof into two steps.

Step
1. Case
k=0
: proof that for all
0\leq t-s\leq 1
we have

(7.47)

\|\nabla_{x}g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a}\,(t-s)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|g_{s}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}.

Integrating by parts, we can compute

\partial_{s}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}\,=\,-2\int_{\mathbb{X}}g_{s}B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,p}g_{s}\,=\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}\Big(2A^{0}_{\mu_{s},p}+{\operatorname{div}}_{v}(A^{1}_{\mu_{s},p})\Big),

and thus, by definition of
A^{0}_{\mu,p},A^{1}_{\mu,p}
,

\partial_{s}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}\,\geq\,\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}-C_{W,\beta,a}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}.

Similarly, we can easily estimate

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{x}g_{s}|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int|\nabla_{xv}g|^{2}-C_{W,\beta,a}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(|\nabla_{x}g_{s}|^{2}+|g_{s}|^{2}+|\nabla_{x}g_{s}||\nabla_{v}g_{s}|\Big),

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}^{2}g_{s}|^{2}-2\int_{\mathbb{X}}\nabla_{v}g_{s}\cdot\nabla_{x}g_{s}-C_{W,\beta,a}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}+|g_{s}|^{2}\Big),

\displaystyle-\partial_{s}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\nabla_{x}g_{s}\cdot\nabla_{v}g_{s}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{x}g_{s}|^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{xv}g_{s}||\nabla_{v}^{2}g_{s}|-C_{W,\beta,a}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\Big(|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}+|g_{s}|^{2}\Big).

Let us consider the functional

(7.48)

F_{s}(g)\,:=\,a_{0}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g|^{2}+a_{1}(t-s)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}g|^{2}+a_{2}(t-s)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{x}g|^{2}-2a_{3}(t-s)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\nabla_{x}g\cdot\nabla_{v}g,

where the constants
a_{0},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}>0
will be suitably chosen in a moment.
In these terms, using Young’s inequality,
the above inequalities lead us to deduce for all
0\leq t-s\leq 1
and
0<\varepsilon,\delta\leq 1
,

\partial_{s}F_{s}(g_{s})\,\geq\,\Big(a_{0}-C_{W,\beta,a}(\delta^{-1}a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3})\Big)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}+\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}a_{3}-\delta a_{1}-C_{W,\beta,a}a_{2}\Big)(t-s)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{x}g_{s}|^{2}\\
+\big(a_{1}-\varepsilon^{-1}a_{3}\big)(t-s)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}^{2}g_{s}|^{2}+\big(a_{2}-\varepsilon a_{3}\big)(t-s)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{xv}g_{s}|^{2}\\
-C_{W,\beta,a}\big(a_{0}+a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}\big)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}.

Choosing for instance
a_{0}=2C_{W,\beta,a}(\delta^{-1}a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3})
,
a_{1}=4a_{3}^{2}
,
a_{2}=1
,
a_{3}=4C_{W,\beta,a}
,
\varepsilon=(2a_{3})^{-1}
, and
\delta=(32a_{3})^{-1}
, we obtain

(7.49)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}F_{s}(g_{s})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}a_{0}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}g_{s}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{8}a_{3}(t-s)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{x}g_{s}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}a_{1}(t-s)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}^{2}g_{s}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}(t-s)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{xv}g_{s}|^{2}-2a_{0}C_{W,\beta,a}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-2a_{0}C_{W,\beta,a}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|g_{s}|^{2}.

By definition of
F_{s}
, as the choice of
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}
satisfies
2a_{3}=\sqrt{a_{1}a_{2}}
, we have

(7.50)

F_{s}(g)\,\geq\,a_{0}\|g\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}a_{1}(t-s)\|\nabla_{v}g\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}(t-s)^{3}\|\nabla_{x}g\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2},

so that the above estimate (
7.49
) entails

\partial_{s}F_{s}(g_{s})\,\gtrsim_{W,\beta,a}\,-F_{s}(g_{s}).

By Grönwall’s inequality with
F_{t}(g)=a_{0}\|g\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}
, this yields for all
0\leq t-s\leq 1
,

F_{s}(g_{s})\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,a}\,\|g_{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})},

and the claim (
7.47
) then follows from (
7.50
).

Step
2. Conclusion.

Given multi-indices
\alpha,\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, we set for abbreviation
J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}:=\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}g_{s}
, which satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{s}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}=-B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,p}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}-r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma},\quad\text{for $0\leq s\leq t$},\\
J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|_{s=t}=\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}g_{t},\end{array}\right.

where the remainder term is given by

r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\,:=\,[\nabla_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma},B_{\mu_{s}}^{*,p}]g_{s}.

Repeating the proof of (
7.49
), for
F_{s}
defined in (
7.48
) with the same choice of constants
a_{0},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}
as in Step 1, we get for all
0\leq t-s\leq 1
,

\partial_{s}F_{s}(J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma})\,\geq\,\tfrac{1}{2}a_{0}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{8}a_{3}(t-s)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{x}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{2}\\
+\tfrac{1}{2}a_{1}(t-s)\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{v}^{2}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}(t-s)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|\nabla_{xv}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{2}-2a_{0}C_{W,\beta,a}\int_{\mathbb{X}}|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}|^{2}\\
-2a_{0}\int_{\mathbb{X}}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}-2a_{1}(t-s)\int_{\mathbb{X}}\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\cdot\nabla_{v}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}-a_{2}(t-s)^{3}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\nabla_{x}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\cdot\nabla_{x}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\\
+2a_{3}(t-s)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\nabla_{x}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\cdot\nabla_{v}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}+2a_{3}(t-s)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}}\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\cdot\nabla_{x}r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}.

Recalling that the remainder term
r_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}
can be written as in (
7.35
) with
\omega
replaced by
\langle z\rangle
,
integrating by parts, and using Young’s inequality to absorb all factors involving
J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}
into the dissipation terms, we deduce for all
0\leq t-s\leq 1
,

\partial_{s}F_{s}(J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma})\,\gtrsim_{W,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,a}\,-\|J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\\
-\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}\Big(\|J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}+(t-s)\|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}+(t-s)^{3}\|\nabla_{x}J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\bigg)\\
-\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}\Big(\|J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}+(t-s)\|\nabla_{v}J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}+(t-s)^{3}\|\nabla_{x}J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}\bigg).

By (
7.50
), this entails

\partial_{s}F_{s}(J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma})\,\gtrsim_{W,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,a}\,-F_{s}(J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma})-\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}F_{s}(J_{s}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}})-\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}F_{s}(J_{s}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}}),

and thus, by Grönwall’s inequality with
F_{t}(g)=a_{0}\|g\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}^{2}
, we deduce for all
0\leq t-s\leq 1
,

F_{s}(J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma})\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,a}\,\|\nabla^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{v}^{\gamma}g_{t}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{X})}+\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\max_{(\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})<(\alpha,\gamma)}F_{u}(J_{u}^{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}})+\max_{i:e_{i}\leq\gamma}F_{u}(J_{u}^{\alpha+e_{i},\gamma-e_{i}})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}u.

By a direct iteration, this proves for all
0\leq t-s\leq 1
,

F_{s}(J_{s}^{\alpha,\gamma})\,\lesssim_{W,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,a}\,\|g_{t}\|_{H^{|\alpha|+|\gamma|}(\mathbb{X})},

and the conclusion (
7.45
) then follows from (
7.50
).
∎

Appendix A
Proof of preliminary results

In this appendix, for convenience, we include proofs of several preliminary results stated in Section
2
. Most of these results are well known but cannot be found as such in the literature.

A.1.
Proof of Lemma
2.5

We follow
[
83
, Proposition 2.2]
, extending it to the present multivariate setting.
Let

M_{X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})\,:=\,\mathbb{E}\big[e^{\sum_{j=1}^{m}t_{j}X_{j}}\big]

be the multivariate moment generating function of
X_{1},\dots,X_{m}
.
We can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{1}\ldots X_{m}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}^{m}}{\mathrm{d}t_{1}\dots\mathrm{d}t_{m}}M_{X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})\bigg|_{t_{1}=\ldots=t_{m}=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}^{m-1}}{\mathrm{d}t_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}t_{m}}\bigg(\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t_{1}}\log M_{X_{1},\dots,X_{m}}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})\Big)M_{X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n})\bigg)\bigg|_{t_{1}=\ldots=t_{m}=0},

and thus, by the Leibniz rule,

\mathbb{E}[X_{1}\ldots X_{m}]\,=\,\sum_{J\subset\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{d}^{\sharp J+1}}{\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{J}}\log M_{X_{1}\ldots X_{m}}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})\bigg)\bigg|_{t_{1}=\ldots=t_{m}=0}\\
\times\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{d}^{m-1-\sharp J}}{\mathrm{d}t_{\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus J}}M_{X_{1}\ldots X_{m}}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})\bigg)\bigg|_{t_{1}=\ldots=t_{m}=0},

where
\mathrm{d}t_{J}
stands for
\mathrm{d}t_{j_{1}}\ldots\mathrm{d}t_{j_{s}}
if
J=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}\}
.
By definition of cumulants and of the moment generating function, this yields the conclusion.∎

A.2.
Proof of Lemma
2.6

We start from the relation between cumulants and moments, cf. (
2.7
):
using similar notation as in (
1.17
), the following general formula holds for all
m\geq 1
, for any bounded random variable
X
,

(A.1)

\kappa^{m}[X]\,=\,\sum_{\pi\vdash\llbracket m\rrbracket}(-1)^{\sharp\pi-1}(\sharp\pi-1)!\prod_{A\in\pi}\mathbb{E}[X^{\sharp A}].

We apply this to a linear functional of the empirical measure: given
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{X})
,

\kappa^{m}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{N}_{t}\bigg]\,=\,\sum_{\pi\vdash\llbracket m\rrbracket}(-1)^{\sharp\pi-1}(\sharp\pi-1)!\prod_{A\in\pi}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{N}_{t}\Big)^{\sharp A}\right].

In the right-hand side, moments of the empirical measure
\mu^{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{Z^{i,N}}
can be computed as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{N}_{t}\Big)^{n}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{n}}\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}\phi(Z^{i_{\ell},N}_{t})\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{n}}\sum_{\pi\vdash\llbracket n\rrbracket}N(N-1)\ldots(N-\sharp\pi+1)\int_{\mathbb{X}^{\sharp\pi}}\Big(\bigotimes_{A\in\pi}\phi^{\sharp A}\Big)F^{\sharp\pi,N}_{t},

while marginals of
F_{N}
can be expressed in terms of correlations via the cluster expansion (
1.16
),

F^{n,N}_{t}(z_{\llbracket n\rrbracket})\,=\,\sum_{\pi\vdash\llbracket n\rrbracket}\prod_{A\in\pi}G^{\sharp A,N}_{t}(z_{A}).

Combining those different identities, after straightforward simplifications, we obtain the following expression for cumulants of the empirical measure in terms of correlation functions,

(A.2)

\kappa^{m}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{X}}\phi\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{N}_{t}\bigg]\,=\,\sum_{\pi\vdash\llbracket m\rrbracket}N^{\sharp\pi-m}\sum_{\rho\vdash\pi}K_{N}(\rho)\int_{\mathbb{X}^{\sharp\pi}}\Big(\bigotimes_{B\in\pi}\phi^{\sharp B}\Big)\Big(\bigotimes_{D\in\rho}G^{\sharp D,N}_{t}(z_{D})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}z_{\pi},

where the coefficients are given by

K_{N}(\rho)\,:=\,\sum_{\sigma\vdash\rho}(-1)^{\sharp\sigma-1}(\sharp\sigma-1)!\Big(\prod_{C\in\sigma}(1-\tfrac{1}{N})\ldots\big(1-\tfrac{(\sum_{D\in C}\sharp D)-1}{N}\big)\Big).

Isolating
\int_{\mathbb{X}^{m}}\phi^{\otimes(m)}G^{m,N}_{t}
in the right-hand side of (
A.2
) (this term is obtained for the choice
\pi=\{\{1\},\ldots,\{m\}\}
and
\rho=\{\pi\}
), and noting that
|K_{N}(\rho)|\leq C_{m}N^{1-\sharp\rho}
, the conclusion follows.
∎

A.3.
Proof of Proposition
2.8

We have shown in
[
43
]
that cumulants can be expressed as polynomials of Glauber derivatives.
Let us first check how this can be extended to the multivariate case, that is, to joint cumulants of families of random variables.
For that purpose, we first introduce some notation and introduce a suitable notion of so-called Stein kernels
\{\Gamma_{n}\}_{n}
generalizing the one in
[
43
]
. For all
n\geq 1
, given bounded
\sigma((Z_{\circ}^{j,N})_{j})
-measurable random variables
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
, we define for all
j\in\llbracket N\rrbracket
,

\delta_{j}^{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})\,:=\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{j\prime}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-(X_{i})^{j\prime})\bigg],

where for all
i,j
the random variable
(X_{i})^{j\prime}
is obtained from
X_{i}
by replacing the underlying variable
Z^{j,N}_{\circ}
by an i.i.d. copy, and where
\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{j\prime}
stands for expectation with respect to this i.i.d. copy.
Note in particular that
\delta_{j}^{1}(X_{1})=D_{j}^{\circ}X_{1}
, while
\delta_{j}^{n}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
should be compared to
\prod_{i=1}^{n}(D_{j}^{\circ}X_{i})
.
In these terms, we now define the Stein kernels

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}(X_{1})\,:=\,\Gamma_{0}^{0}(X_{1})\,:=\,X_{1},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{2})\,:=\,\Gamma_{1}^{1}(X_{1},X_{2})\,:=\,\sum_{j=1}^{N}(D_{j}^{\circ}X_{2})\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}(D_{j}^{\circ}X_{1})\,=\,\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta^{1}_{j}(X_{2})\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}(D_{j}^{\circ}X_{1}),

and iteratively, for all
n\geq 1
,
m\geq 0
, and
\sharp J=m
,

\Gamma_{n}^{n+m}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+1},X_{J})\,:=\,\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Big(\delta_{j}^{m+1}(X_{n+1},X_{J})\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}D_{j}\Gamma_{n-1}^{n-1}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})\\[-5.69054pt]
-\tfrac{1}{m+2}\mathds{1}_{n>1}\Gamma_{n-1}^{n+m}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+1},X_{J})\Big),

where we let
X_{J}=(X_{j_{1}},\ldots,X_{j_{s}})
for
J=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{s}\}
,
and we then set

\Gamma_{n}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+1})\,:=\,\Gamma^{n}_{n}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+1}).

Note that
\Gamma_{n}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+1})
is not symmetric in its arguments
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+1}
(we could choose to consider instead its symmetrization, but it does not matter here).
In these terms, we can now state the following representation formula for cumulants.

Lemma A.1
.

For all
n\geq 0
and all bounded
\sigma((Z_{\circ}^{j,N})_{j})
-measurable random variables
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+1}
, we have

\kappa^{n+1}_{\circ}[X_{1},\dots,X_{n+1}]\,=\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[\Gamma_{n}(X_{1},\dots,X_{n+1})\big].

Proof.

We omit the subscript ‘
\circ
’
for notational simplicity.
By the Hellfer-Sjöstrand representation formula (
2.11
), we can write

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{1}]\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]+\operatorname{Cov}\bigg[X_{1},\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{1}]\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[D_{j}\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}\bigg)\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}D_{j}X_{1}\bigg].

Now note that the following formula is easily obtained by induction for differences of products: for all
(a_{i})_{2\leq i\leq m},(b_{i})_{2\leq i\leq m}\subset\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\prod_{i=2}^{m}a_{i}-\prod_{i=2}^{m}b_{i}\,=\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\\
J\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\sharp J+1}\bigg(\prod_{i\not\in J}a_{i}\bigg)\bigg(\prod_{i\in J}(a_{i}-b_{i})\bigg),

and this obviously implies

(A.4)

D_{j}\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}\bigg)\,=\,\mathbb{E}^{j\prime}\bigg[\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}-\prod_{i=2}^{m}(X_{i})^{j\prime}\bigg]\,=\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\\
J\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\sharp J+1}\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus J}X_{i}\Big)\,\delta_{j}^{\sharp J}(X_{J}).

Inserting this into (
A.3
),
separating the contributions of singletons in the sum,
and recognizing the definition of
\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1}
,
we get

(A.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{0}(X_{1})]\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\\
J\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\sharp J+1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus J}X_{k}\Big)\,\delta_{j}^{\sharp J}(X_{J})\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}D_{j}X_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{0}(X_{1})]\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]+\sum_{\ell\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}}X_{i}\Big)\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{\ell})\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}\\
\sharp J\geq 1\end{subarray}}\tfrac{(-1)^{\sharp J}}{\sharp J+1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus(\{\ell\}\cup J)}X_{i}\Big)\Gamma_{1}^{\sharp J+1}(X_{1},X_{\ell},X_{J})\bigg].

Using again the Hellfer–Sjöstrand representation formula (
2.11
) to handle the second right-hand side term, we can decompose for all
\ell\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}}X_{i}\Big)\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{\ell})\bigg]\,=\,\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{\ell})]\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}}X_{i}\bigg]\\
+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[D_{j}\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}}X_{i}\Big)\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}D_{j}\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{\ell})\bigg],

and thus, appealing again to (
A.4
) to reformulate the last term,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}}X_{i}\Big)\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{\ell})\bigg]\,=\,\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{\ell})]\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}}X_{i}\bigg]\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}\\
J\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\sharp J+1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus(\{\ell\}\cup J)}X_{i}\Big)\delta^{\sharp J}_{j}(X_{J})\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}D_{j}\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{\ell})\Big].

Inserting this into (
A.5
) and recognizing the definition of
\Gamma_{2}
,
we find

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]\,=\,\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{0}(X_{1})]\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=2}^{m}X_{i}\bigg]+\sum_{\ell\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket}\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{1}(X_{1},X_{\ell})]\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell\}}X_{i}\bigg]\\
+\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}}X_{i}\Big)\Gamma_{2}(X_{1},X_{\ell},X_{\ell^{\prime}})\bigg]\\
+\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset[2,m]\setminus\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}\\
J\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\tfrac{(-1)^{\sharp J}}{\sharp J+1}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\prod_{i\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket\setminus(\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}\cup J)}X_{i}\Big)\Gamma_{2}^{\sharp J+2}(X_{1},X_{\ell},X_{\ell^{\prime}},X_{J})\bigg],

and the claim follows by iteration and a direct comparison with the formula (
2.9
).
∎

We can now turn to the proof of Proposition
2.8
: the above representation formula for cumulants implies that cumulants can indeed be controlled as desired in terms of higher-order Glauber derivatives.

Proof of Proposition
2.8
.

First note that Jensen’s inequality yields for all
m\geq 0
and
r\geq 1
,

\|\delta^{m}_{j}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{r}(\Omega_{\circ})}\,=\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\Big|\mathbb{E}^{j\prime}_{\circ}\Big[\prod_{i=1}^{m}(X_{i}-(X_{i})^{j\prime})\Big]\Big|^{r}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{r}}\,\leq\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\mathbb{E}^{j\prime}_{\circ}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{m}|X_{i}-(X_{i})^{j\prime}|^{r}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{r}},

and thus, decomposing
X_{i}-(X_{i})^{j\prime}=D_{j}^{\circ}X_{i}-(D_{j}^{\circ}X_{i})^{j\prime}
and using Hölder’s inequality

\|\delta^{m}_{j}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{r}(\Omega_{\circ})}\,\leq\,\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}^{j^{\prime}}\Big[\big|D^{\circ}_{j}X_{i}-(D^{\circ}_{j}X_{i})^{j^{\prime}}\big|^{rm}\Big]^{\frac{1}{rm}}\,\leq\,2^{m}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\|D_{j}^{\circ}X_{i}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{rm}(\Omega_{\circ})}.

By induction, using this estimate along with (
2.10
), we find for all
m,n,r
, for all bounded
\sigma((Z_{\circ}^{j,N})_{j})
-measurable random variables
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n+m+1}
,

\|\Gamma^{m+n}_{n}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m+n+1})\|_{\operatorname{L}^{r}(\Omega)}\\
\,\lesssim_{m,n,r}\,\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}N^{k+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},\dots,a_{m+n+1}\geq 1\\
\sum_{j}a_{j}=m+n+k+1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{m+n+1}\|(D^{\circ})^{a_{j}}X_{j}\|_{\ell^{\infty}_{\neq}\big(\operatorname{L}^{\frac{r}{a_{j}}(m+n+k+1)}(\Omega_{\circ})\big)}.

Combined with the representation formula of Lemma
A.1
, this yields the conclusion.
∎

A.4.
Proof of Proposition
2.9

By homogeneity, it suffices to consider a bounded random variable
Y
with

\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[Y]\,=\,0,\qquad\sigma_{Y}^{2}\,=\,\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[Y]\,=\,1.

Given
g\in C^{1}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
, we define its Stein transform
S_{g}
as the solution of Stein’s equation

(A.6)

S_{g}^{\prime}(x)-xS_{g}(x)=g(x)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}[g(\mathcal{N})].

As shown in
[
89
]
, the latter can be computed as

S_{g}(x)\,=\,-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{t}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\Big[g^{\prime}\big(\sqrt{t}\,x+\sqrt{1-t}\,\mathcal{N}\big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}t,

Using this formula and a Gaussian integration by parts, we easily obtain the following bound,

(A.7)

\|S_{g}^{\prime}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\,\lesssim\,\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Evaluating equation (
A.6
) at
Y
and taking the expectation, we find

\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[g(Y)]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}[g(\mathcal{N})]\,=\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\big[S_{g}^{\prime}(Y)-YS_{g}(Y)\big].

Now appealing to the Helffer–Sjöstrand representation formula of Lemma
2.7
(iii) for the covariance
\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[YS_{g}(Y)]=\operatorname{Cov}_{\circ}[Y,S_{g}(Y)]
, this yields

(A.8)

\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[g(Y)]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}[g(\mathcal{N})]\,=\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[S_{g}^{\prime}(Y)-\sum_{j=1}^{N}(D^{\circ}_{j}S_{g}(Y))\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}(D^{\circ}_{j}Y)\bigg].

A Taylor expansion gives for all
p\geq 1
,

\big\|D^{\circ}_{j}S_{g}(Y)-S_{g}^{\prime}(Y)D^{\circ}_{j}Y\big\|_{\operatorname{L}^{p}(\Omega_{\circ})}\,\leq\,\|S_{g}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|D^{\circ}_{j}Y\|_{\operatorname{L}^{2p}(\Omega_{\circ})}^{2}.

Using this to replace
D^{\circ}_{j}S_{g}(Y)
in (
A.8
), using Hölder’s inequality with
p=\frac{3}{2}
to bound the error, using the boundedness of
\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}
in
\operatorname{L}^{3}(\Omega_{\circ})
, cf. Lemma
2.7
(ii), and recalling the bound (
A.7
) on the Stein transform, we are led to

\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[g(Y)]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}[g(\mathcal{N})]\,\lesssim\,\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\bigg[\Big|1-\sum_{j=1}^{N}(D^{\circ}_{j}Y)\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}(D^{\circ}_{j}Y)\Big|\bigg]+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[|D^{\circ}_{j}Y|^{3}]\bigg).

Now recalling the Helffer–Sjöstrand representation formula of Lemma
2.7
(iii) in form of

1\,=\,\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}[Y]\,=\,\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{N}(D^{\circ}_{j}Y)\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}(D^{\circ}_{j}Y)\Big],

we deduce by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[g(Y)]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}}[g(\mathcal{N})]\,\lesssim\,\|g^{\prime\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\bigg(\operatorname{Var}_{\circ}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{N}(D^{\circ}_{j}Y)\mathcal{L}_{\circ}^{-1}(D^{\circ}_{j}Y)\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[|D^{\circ}_{j}Y|^{3}]\bigg).

Taking the supremum over
g\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R})
, the conclusion follows in the second-order Zolotarev distance
\mathrm{d}_{2}
. The proof in the
1
-Wasserstein distance can be obtained in the same way by noting that on top of (
A.7
) the Stein transform also satisfies
\|S_{g}^{\prime}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|g^{\prime}\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
, cf.
[
89
]
. The proof in Kolmogorov distance is more delicate and we refer to
[
71
, Theorem 4.2]
.
∎

A.5.
Proof of Proposition
2.10

We appeal to the following degraded version of a log-Sobolev inequality as obtained in
[
44
, Proposition 2.4]
: for all random variables
Y\in\operatorname{L}^{2}(\Omega_{\circ})
, we have

\operatorname{Ent}_{\circ}[Y^{2}]\,\leq\,2\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\Big[\sup_{j}\,(Y-Y^{j})^{2}\Big],

where we recall that
Y^{j}
stands for the random variable obtained from
Y
by replacing the underlying variable
Z_{\circ}^{j,N}
by an i.i.d. copy,
and where
\sup_{j}
stands for the essential supremum with respect to this i.i.d. copy.
Applying this inequality to
Y=e^{\frac{1}{2}X}
, using the bound

|e^{\frac{1}{2}X}-e^{\frac{1}{2}X^{j}}|\,\leq\,\tfrac{1}{2}|X-X^{j}|\int_{0}^{1}e^{\frac{1}{2}(X-t(X-X^{j}))}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\leq\,\tfrac{1}{2}e^{\frac{1}{2}X}|X-X^{j}|\,e^{\frac{1}{2}|X-X^{j}|},

we find

\operatorname{Ent}_{\circ}[e^{X}]\,\leq\,\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}\Big[e^{X}\sup_{j}\,(X-X^{j})^{2}e^{|X-X^{j}|}\Big]\,\leq\,\tfrac{N}{2}M_{X}^{2}e^{M_{X}}\mathbb{E}_{\circ}[e^{X}],

in terms of

M_{X}\,:=\,\sup_{1\leq j\leq N}\operatorname{sup\,ess}|X-X^{j}|\,\leq\,2\sup_{1\leq j\leq N}\operatorname{sup\,ess}|D_{j}^{\circ}X|\,\leq\,L.

We are now in position to appeal to Herbst’s argument in the form of
[
74
, Proposition 2.9 and Corollary 2.12]
and the conclusion follows.
∎

A.6.
Proof of Lemma
2.12

We focus on the Langevin setting for shortness.
In addition, we only prove the estimates for
\mu_{t}^{N}
, that is, (
2.15
), (
2.16
), and (
2.17
), while the corresponding estimates for the mean-field dynamics are skipped: they can be obtained similarly, letting
N=1
and replacing sums of interaction forces by mean-field force in the stochastic estimates below.
We split the proof into three steps.

Step
1. Proof of (
2.15
).

In the spirit of
[
11
]
, we consider the random process

G_{t}^{N}\,:=\,\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big(1+a|X_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}+|V_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}+\eta X_{t}^{i,N}\cdot V_{t}^{i,N}\Big),

for some
\eta\in(0,2\sqrt{a})
to be suitable chosen.
Note that this range of
\eta
ensures

(A.9)

G_{t}^{N}\,\simeq_{a,\eta}\,Q(\mu^{N}_{t})^{2}.

By Itô’s formula, the particle dynamics (
1.1
) yields

(A.10)

\mathrm{d}G_{t}^{N}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Big(-1+a\eta|X_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}+(\beta-\eta)|V_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}+\tfrac{\eta\beta}{2}X_{t}^{i,N}\cdot V_{t}^{i,N}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\\
-\tfrac{\kappa}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}(2V_{t}^{i,N}+\eta X_{t}^{i,N})\cdot\nabla W(X_{t}^{i,N}-X_{t}^{j,N})\,\mathrm{d}t+\tfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(2V_{t}^{i,N}+\eta X_{t}^{i,N})\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{i}.

From this equation
and Itô’s formula, we then find for all
k\geq 1
,

\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{t}^{N})^{k}]\,\leq\,-\tfrac{k}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{B}\bigg[(G_{t}^{N})^{k-1}\Big(1+\tfrac{1}{4}a\eta|X_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}+\big(\tfrac{\beta}{2}-\eta(1+\tfrac{\beta^{2}}{8a})\big)|V_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}\Big)\bigg]\\
+k\big(3+(\tfrac{\eta}{a}+\tfrac{2}{\beta})\|\nabla W\|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big)\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{t}^{N})^{k-1}]\\
+\tfrac{k(k-1)}{2N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[(G_{t}^{N})^{k-2}|2V_{t}^{i,N}+\eta X_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}\big].

Provided that
0<\eta\ll_{\beta,a}1
is small enough (only depending on
\beta,a
), there exist
\lambda,C>0
(only depending on
d,W,\beta,a
) such that we get for all
k\geq 1
,

\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{t}^{N})^{k}]\,\leq\,-\lambda k\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{t}^{N})^{k}]+Ck(1+\tfrac{k}{N})\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{t}^{N})^{k-1}],

and thus, by Grönwall’s inequality,

\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{t}^{N})^{k}]\,\leq\,e^{-\lambda kt}\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{0}^{N})^{k}]+Ck(1+\tfrac{k}{N})\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda k(t-s)}\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{s}^{N})^{k-1}]\,\mathrm{d}s.

A direct induction then yields for all
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{t}^{N})^{k}]\,\leq\,\sum_{j=0}^{k}e^{-\lambda jt}\,\tfrac{k!}{j!}(\tfrac{1}{\lambda}C)^{k-j}(1+\tfrac{k}{N})^{k-j}\,\mathbb{E}_{B}[(G_{0}^{N})^{j}],

and (
2.15
) follows.

Step
2. Proof of (
2.16
).

From (
A.10
) and Itô’s formula, arguing similarly as in Step 1, provided that
0<\eta\ll_{\beta,a}1
is small enough (only depending on
\beta,a
), there exist
\lambda,C>0
(only depending on
d,W,\beta,a
) such that we have for all
t,\alpha,L\geq 0
and
0\leq\delta\leq 1
,

(A.11)

\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big]\,\leq\,-2\lambda\delta\alpha\,\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big]\\
+C(L+1)\delta\alpha\,\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta-1}e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big]+\tfrac{C}{N}\delta^{2}\alpha^{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{2\delta-1}e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big].

For
\delta<1
, choosing
L=(\frac{C\alpha}{\lambda N}+1)^{\frac{1}{1-\delta}}
,
we get
\frac{C}{N}\delta^{2}\alpha^{2}(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{2\delta-1}\leq\lambda\delta\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}
, and thus

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\lambda\delta\alpha\,\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big]+C(L+1)\delta\alpha\,\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta-1}e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\lambda\delta\alpha\,\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big]+C_{\delta}\alpha(\tfrac{\alpha}{N}+1)^{\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}}\,\mathbb{E}_{B}\big[e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}\big].

This amounts to the following differential inequality for the Laplace transform
F_{\delta}(t,\alpha):=\mathbb{E}_{B}[e^{\alpha(G_{t}^{N}+L)^{\delta}}]
: for all
t,\alpha\geq 0
,

\partial_{t}F_{\delta}(t,\alpha)+\lambda\delta\alpha\partial_{\alpha}F_{\delta}(t,\alpha)\,\leq\,C_{\delta}\alpha(\tfrac{\alpha}{N}+1)^{\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}}F_{\delta}(t,\alpha),

which can be rewritten as

\partial_{t}[F_{\delta}(t,e^{\lambda\delta t}\alpha)]\,\leq\,C_{\delta}(e^{\lambda\delta t}\alpha)(\tfrac{e^{\lambda\delta t}\alpha}{N}+1)^{\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}}F_{\delta}(t,e^{\lambda\delta t}\alpha).

By integration, this yields for all
t,\alpha\geq 0
,

F_{\delta}(t,\alpha)\,\leq\,\exp\Big(C_{\delta}\alpha(\tfrac{\alpha}{N}+1)^{\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}}\Big)F_{\delta}(0,e^{-\lambda\delta t}\alpha),

and (
2.16
) follows after recalling
G^{N}_{t}\simeq_{\beta,a}Q(\mu^{N}_{t})^{2}
.

Step
3. Proof of (
2.17
).

Starting point is (
A.11
) with
\delta=1
and
L=0
: provided that
0<\eta\ll_{\beta,a}1
is small enough (only depending on
\beta,a
), there exist
\lambda,C>0
(only depending on
d,W,\beta,a
) such that we have for all
t,\alpha>0
,

\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}[e^{\alpha G_{t}^{N}}]\,\leq\,-2\lambda\alpha\mathbb{E}[G_{t}^{N}e^{\alpha G_{t}^{N}}]+C\alpha\mathbb{E}[e^{\alpha G_{t}^{N}}]+\tfrac{C}{N}\alpha^{2}\mathbb{E}[G_{t}^{N}e^{\alpha G_{t}^{N}}].

Hence, setting
\alpha_{0}:=\frac{\lambda}{C}
, we get for
\alpha\leq N\alpha_{0}
,

\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}[e^{\alpha G_{t}^{N}}]\,\leq\,-\lambda\alpha\mathbb{E}[G_{t}^{N}e^{\alpha G_{t}^{N}}]+C\alpha\mathbb{E}[e^{\alpha G_{t}^{N}}].

This amounts to the following differential inequality for the Laplace transform
F(t,\alpha):=\mathbb{E}[e^{\alpha G_{t}^{N}}]
: for all
t\geq 0
and
0\leq\alpha\leq N\alpha_{0}
,

\partial_{t}F(t,\alpha)+\lambda\alpha\partial_{\alpha}F(t,\alpha)\,\leq\,C\alpha F(t,\alpha),

which can be rewritten as follows, for all
t\geq 0
and
0\leq e^{\lambda t}\alpha\leq N\alpha_{0}
,

\partial_{t}[F(t,e^{\lambda t}\alpha)]\leq Ce^{\lambda t}\alpha F(t,e^{\lambda t}\alpha).

This yields
F(t,\alpha)\leq e^{\alpha C/\lambda}F(0,e^{-\lambda t}\alpha)
for
t\geq 0
and
0\leq\alpha\leq N\alpha_{0}
,
and (
2.17
) follows.
∎
